# Plan 234 — Transparent networking over vsock

**Status:** In progress — `mvm-net` protocol contract, guest bridge planning, Linux guest executor, packet translation foundation, dependency-light guest pump seam, TCP response synthesis, bounded pump loop substrate, concrete TUN/wire adapters, Linux guest runner, packaged guest bridge binary, dependency-light host authority core, host runner/connector/policy-adapter substrate, host authority process contract, backend host authority spawn, backend port-5254 relay, upstream TCP return-path draining, guest TCP half-close preservation, workload-path HVF runner selector cutover, gated live CLI smoke harness, source-checkout smoke local-kernel hardening, live HVF DNS/TCP proof through `mvm-guest-netd` plus `mvm-host-netd`, C1 DNS/TCP MVP, C1 deny/no-network seam coverage, the first C2 TLS-transform contract slice, the allowlist-hostname TLS terminate/re-originate substrate, a feature-gated guest-facing TLS backend seam (`rustls` default, opt-in `boring` compatibility path), the ACK-only host-to-guest return path for admitted guest TCP data, live BusyBox HTTPS/deny/no-network public-path proof, fail-closed transformed-TLS ALPN/plugin-chain admission, explicit guest transformed-certificate rejection detail, the first decrypted-stream plugin slice (`audit`) with built-in manifests/budgets and activity audit detail, the first guest-to-upstream managed-secret placeholder substitution slice (`secret-replacement`) over decrypted plaintext, the first guest-to-upstream metadata-endpoint guard slice (`metadata-endpoint-deny`) over decrypted plaintext request heads, baseline transformed-`443` guest-route plugin-chain wiring for `audit` plus `metadata-endpoint-deny`, the first mutating upstream-to-guest guard slice (`response-leak-guard`) that redacts known secret values out of upstream plaintext before the guest sees them, the first admitted ICMP echo executor plus gated public-path smoke coverage, and broader admitted UDP datagram forwarding plus guest return-path synthesis landed.

**Owner directive:** workload networking remains a vsock-mediated capability. The guest gets normal application-visible networking behavior, but the host remains the only authority that opens real network sockets, performs DNS, terminates TLS for secret-bearing flows, applies egress policy, records audit, and emits attestable evidence.

## Decisions

- Use one crate, `crates/mvm-net`, for the transparent networking subsystem. Keep protocol, guest bridge, host authority, TLS transform, plugin pipeline, DNS, ICMP echo, limits, and audit modules together until their interfaces are stable enough to extract.
- Keep compile-time cost explicit: the default crate build exposes protocol types without serialization derives or runtime dependencies; heavier support such as serde, guest TUN execution, host async I/O, TLS, and plugins must remain opt-in features.
- The existing `mvm-network` crate remains the low-level provisioning/provider trait surface. It is not renamed or overloaded.
- TLS termination is mandatory for any flow that can carry host-held secrets, replacement material, or response bytes that must be checked before the guest sees them.
- Replacement is plugin-like, but plugins operate on semantic stream events and bounded chunks. Byte-by-byte callbacks are not the hot-path API.
- ICMP echo is supported as a controlled protocol adapter. Arbitrary raw sockets are out of scope for the production default.
- The first production target is transparent DNS plus TCP over vsock for ordinary guest tools such as `wget`, package managers, SDK clients, and browser automation.

## Workstreams

- [x] **A1 — Create the single `mvm-net` crate and protocol foundation.**
      Add the workspace member, keep the first implementation to tested shared protocol primitives, keep serde opt-in, document the crate in the architecture reference, and verify the default normal dependency tree stays empty beyond `mvm-net`.
- [x] **A2 — Define the complete protocol contract.**
      Extend `mvm-net::proto` with version negotiation, flow correlation, DNS, TCP, UDP, ICMP echo, TLS-transform routing, denial reasons, and audit correlation fields. Add serde roundtrip and malformed-message tests for every message family.
- [x] **B1 — Build the guest network bridge.**
      Add the guest-side module and binary that creates `tun0`, installs the synthetic default route, points resolver configuration at the synthetic gateway, and translates guest traffic into `mvm-net` protocol frames over vsock.
- [x] **B1a — Add dependency-light guest bridge planning.**
      Add `mvm-net::guest` behind an opt-in `guest` feature with validated synthetic IPv4 defaults, interface-name validation, resolver paths, transparent-network vsock port, and an ordered operation plan for TUN, default route, resolver, and vsock-authority connection. Keep normal dependencies empty for default, no-default-features, and `guest` builds.
- [x] **B1b — Implement the Linux guest TUN executor.**
      Add the Linux-only executor that opens `/dev/net/tun`, applies the B1a operation plan, installs route/resolver state, connects to the vsock authority, and fails closed on missing capabilities or kernel support.
- [x] **B1c — Implement the TUN packet/protocol pump.**
      Translate IP packets read from the guest TUN fd into DNS, TCP, UDP, and ICMP `mvm-net` protocol frames over the vsock authority stream, apply host responses back to the TUN device, and enforce bounded buffers/backpressure before any guest packet can escape.
- [x] **B1c1 — Add dependency-light packet translation foundation.**
      Add `mvm-net::guest_packet` behind the existing `guest` feature. Parse outbound IPv4 from the TUN boundary into DNS query, TCP open/data/close, UDP datagram, and ICMP echo protocol events; track DNS/ICMP/flow correlation state; remember synthetic DNS IP mappings; synthesize DNS and ICMP echo replies back into IPv4 packets; and refuse malformed, fragmented, unsupported, over-limit, and unknown-flow packets without adding runtime dependencies.
- [x] **B1c2a — Add the dependency-light guest pump seam.**
      Add `mvm-net::guest_pump` behind the existing `guest` feature. Wire the packet translator to generic authority and packet-sink traits; send outbound translated `mvm-net` protocol messages to the authority; write synthesized DNS and ICMP echo responses back to the guest sink; ignore handshake control messages; and keep unsupported transport authority responses fail-closed until protocol-specific synthesis lands.
- [x] **B1c2b — Add stateful TCP response synthesis.**
      Extend `mvm-net::guest_packet` with TCP flow state keyed by both guest 4-tuple and `FlowId`; synthesize authority `TcpOpenResult` messages into SYN-ACK or RST-ACK packets; synthesize ordered host-to-guest `TcpData` chunks into PSH/ACK or FIN/ACK packets; synthesize host closes into FIN/RST packets; reject wrong-direction, out-of-order, unknown-flow, and over-limit chunks; and wire `mvm-net::guest_pump` to write those TCP packets back to the guest sink.
- [x] **B1c2c — Add bounded guest pump loop substrate.**
      Extend `mvm-net::guest_pump` with a reusable `GuestPumpLoop` and `GuestPumpLoopConfig`; own one bounded TUN packet buffer; add testable source/authority-receive traits; process at most one guest packet and a bounded number of authority messages per tick; report idle/closed edges; reject source errors, invalid read sizes, zero limits, and over-IPv4 packet buffers without adding dependencies.
- [x] **B1c2d — Add the concrete Linux TUN/vsock fd adapters.**
      Add `TunPacketIo` for the Linux TUN packet source/sink boundary and an opt-in `wire-json` feature with bounded length-prefixed `NetMessage` framing, partial-read preservation, guest authority trait impls, closed-stream handling, and oversized-frame refusal.
- [x] **B1c2e — Add the concrete Linux TUN/vsock runner.**
      Add the real runner that reads from the Linux TUN fd, writes protocol frames to the vsock authority stream, consumes host responses, synthesizes TCP replies to the guest, and enforces bounded readiness/backpressure across both directions.
- [x] **B1d — Package the guest bridge binary.**
      Add the lean guest entrypoint that loads its bridge config, runs the Linux executor plus guest runner, reports startup/runtime failures clearly, and can be embedded into guest images without pulling host-side networking code.
- [x] **B2 — Build the host network authority.**
      Add the host-side module and binary that accepts the per-VM vsock stream, owns synthetic DNS/IP mapping, reuses the canonical network policy projection, opens real host sockets only after admission, and records flow-open/flow-close audit events.
- [x] **B2a — Add the dependency-light host authority core.**
      Add `mvm-net::host` behind an opt-in `host` feature with a pure host-side authority state machine: protocol handshake admission, synthetic A-record mapping, DNS/TCP/UDP/ICMP policy decisions, flow-open/flow-close audit events, and a TCP connector trait that is invoked only after policy admission. Keep default/no-default/host normal dependency trees dependency-light.
- [x] **B2b — Add the concrete host runner and policy adapter.**
      Add the per-VM vsock listener/stream runner, length-prefixed wire integration, canonical `mvm-core` policy projection adapter behind an explicit opt-in feature, real host TCP connector, and host authority binary entrypoint. The runner must open real host sockets only after B2a admission succeeds and must fail closed on audit or policy construction errors.
- [x] **B2b1 — Add host runner, std TCP connector, and canonical policy adapter substrate.**
      Add `mvm-net::host_runner` behind `host-runner` for bounded length-prefixed authority processing; add `host-std` with a standard-library TCP connector that uses a policy-supplied pinned upstream IP when available; add `host-mvm-core` with an opt-in adapter from canonical `mvm-core` network policy projection plus DNS pins to `mvm-net` host admission. Keep the heavy `mvm-core` dependency out of default, `host`, `host-runner`, and `host-std` builds.
- [x] **B2b2 — Add the host authority process and backend listener integration.**
      Add the host daemon entrypoint, per-VM transport accept/dial wiring, policy/pin config loading, structured audit sink, and backend supervisor integration that runs B2b1 over the actual per-VM stream.
- [x] **B2b2a — Add the host authority binary and process contract.**
      Add feature-gated `mvm-host-netd` behind `host-netd`; load explicit JSON config with network policy, DNS pins, timestamp, and bounded runner/wire limits; run the host authority over split stdin/stdout length-prefixed JSON streams; emit structured JSON audit events; and keep the binary plus `mvm-core` policy adapter out of default, `host`, and `host-runner` builds.
- [x] **B2b2b — Wire backend per-VM listener supervision.**
      Spawn or attach `mvm-host-netd` from the VM backend/supervisor path, bind it to the actual per-VM vsock authority stream, generate the admitted policy/pin config, collect its structured audit output, and fail closed if the process, transport, policy projection, or audit path is unavailable.
- [x] **B2b2b1 — Add host authority UDS listener and backend spawn/reap seam.**
      Add `mvm-host-netd --listen-uds` for a per-VM authority stream, generate the host daemon JSON config with admitted network policy and DNS pins, spawn the process through the existing aux-bin resolver, collect structured audit output to a per-VM JSONL file, record/reap its pid and socket, wire the workload runner's production endpoint spawner to the new authority socket, and keep `mvm-backend` from linking `mvm-net`.
- [x] **B2b2b2a — Route guest vsock port 5254 to the host authority stream.**
      Thread the spawned `mvm-host-netd` authority socket through the workload `VmmSpec`, HVF supervisor JSON, `HostChannels`, and in-house virtio-vsock device; relay guest port `5254` byte-for-byte to the authority; and fail closed with `OP_RST` when no authority is wired.
- [x] **B2b2b2b — Package and auto-launch the guest bridge for transparent networking.**
      Include `mvm-guest-netd` in the OCI runtime injection set, source-checkout guest-runtime cache, shipped `mvmctl` embedded binaries, and prebuilt install path; have the injected PID 1 start it only when `mvm.netd=1` is present; and have the HVF boot path append that token only when a transparent-network authority socket is wired.
- [x] **B2b2b2c — Fail closed when requested guest netd cannot stay up.**
      When `mvm.netd=1` is present, make the injected PID 1 treat `mvm-guest-netd` as required: it records the started pid, verifies the process survives startup, and refuses to start the guest agent if netd is missing or exits immediately.
- [x] **B2b2b2d — Add the first in-process guest DNS/TCP acceptance path.**
      Add a `guest,host` feature test that drives raw guest DNS and TCP packets through `GuestBridgePump`, a real `HostAuthority`, and a recording TCP connector, proving synthetic DNS allocation, guest TCP open mapping back to the DNS name, SYN-ACK synthesis, and guest-to-host TCP data forwarding without booting a VM.
- [x] **B2b2b2e — Bound host authority stop/reap behavior.**
      Make `reap_mvm_net_authority` terminate the recorded `mvm-host-netd` pid with SIGTERM, wait for exit, escalate to SIGKILL on timeout, reap child zombies when possible, and remove pid/socket state; cover a SIGTERM-ignoring stub process in unit tests.
- [x] **B2b2b2f — Add the gated live CLI DNS/TCP smoke harness.**
      Add `crates/mvm-cli/tests/transparent_net_e2e.rs` plus `just e2e-transparent-net` so an operator can build the current `mvmctl`, HVF supervisor, and `mvm-host-netd`, then boot `machine run --image ... --allow-host ...` and prove ordinary in-guest DNS plus TCP reaches an admitted public host through `mvm-guest-netd` and `mvm-host-netd`. The original landed defaults targeted `example.com:80`; a later E2a slice retargets the harness to `https://example.com:443` so the public smoke enters the guest-trust/TLS path by default. The test is skipped unless `MVM_TRANSPARENT_NET_SMOKE=1`; it uses a hard watchdog, per-step process-group kill, stable scratch logs under `/tmp`, target-dir helper pinning, and a best-effort bounded `machine stop` cleanup. Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo test -p mvm-cli --test transparent_net_e2e -- --test-threads=1` (3 tests). The live smoke itself remains unrun in this host turn because it boots a microVM and the non-bypass embed build requires Zig 0.13.0.
- [x] **B2b2b2g — Drain admitted upstream TCP response bytes back to the guest.**
      Extend the host TCP connector with a bounded drain API, keep the authority's open-flow bookkeeping alive until the upstream side actually closes, and have the host runner emit admitted host-to-guest TCP data and close events even while the guest wire is otherwise idle. Prove the seam with a real loopback TCP listener driven through `GuestBridgePump`, `HostAuthority`, `StdTcpConnector`, and `host_runner`, showing that upstream response bytes and EOF synthesize guest-visible TCP packets without booting a VM. Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest,host,host-std,host-runner -- --test-threads=1` (76 tests); `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features guest,host,host-std,host-runner -- -D warnings`.
- [x] **B2b2b2h — Preserve guest TCP half-close until the upstream side closes.**
      Translate guest `FIN` as a terminal `TcpData.end_stream` chunk instead of eagerly tearing the flow down, so the host connector only shuts down the upstream write half and the guest can still receive response bytes plus the final close. Prove both the packet-translation seam and a real loopback half-close roundtrip through `GuestBridgePump`, `HostAuthority`, and `StdTcpConnector` without booting a VM. Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest,host,host-std,host-runner -- --test-threads=1` (78 tests); `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features guest,host,host-std,host-runner -- -D warnings`.

Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest,host,host-std,host-runner -- --test-threads=1`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo test -p mvm-cli --test transparent_net_e2e -- --nocapture` (live rerun still required for the remaining B2b2b2 proof).

      Run the gated live microVM DNS/TCP acceptance path through `mvm-guest-netd` and `mvm-host-netd` in the approved microVM environment and fix any failures it exposes. The closeout fixes covered four live-only seams: host-side guest-agent transport now prefers the workload runner's `agent.sock` and falls back to detached-HVF `hvf-agent.sock`; the smoke harness and runtime guest cross-builds both honor isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` so live runs stop reusing stale shared-target guest binaries; OCI PID 1 stages a writable `/run/mvm/resolv.conf`, bind-mounts it over `/etc/resolv.conf` when present, and shadows `/etc` onto tmpfs first when OCI images omit that path entirely; and the guest packet translator now routes any well-formed UDP/53 query through DNS admission so loopback stub-resolver traffic still reaches the authority instead of being denied as raw UDP. MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo test -p mvm-cli --test transparent_net_e2e -- --nocapture`; `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=... MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 MVM_TRANSPARENT_NET_SMOKE=1 cargo test -p mvm-cli --test transparent_net_e2e -- --nocapture` (live green before the HTTPS retarget: `machine_run_allow_host_resolves_dns_and_fetches_http`).

- [x] **C1a — Prove denied/no-network DNS and TCP at the full in-process seam.**
      Extend the `guest,host` acceptance coverage so a deny-all host authority returns a guest-visible refused DNS packet without allocating a synthetic address, and a denied TCP open returns a guest-visible RST without invoking the host connector. Validation: `cargo fmt --all -- --check`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest,host -- --test-threads=1` (54 tests); `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features guest,host -- -D warnings`.
- [x] **C2 — Add mandatory TLS transform for secret-bearing flows.**
      The transformed-TLS contract is now complete for the mandatory secret-bearing lane: the backend stages a per-VM name-constrained guest trust root, `mvm-host-netd` keeps the private key host-side, the authority terminates guest TLS and re-originates upstream TLS, and pinned-client / unsupported-ALPN cases fail closed with explicit guest-certificate-rejection detail where applicable. The broader decrypted-stream plugin surface now lives under `C3`, not `C2`. Validation: `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.
- [x] **C2a — Thread TLS-transform intent through the guest/host TCP contract.**
      Mark synthetic-host `:443` guest opens as `TlsPolicy::RequiredForTransform` with an explicit `TlsTransformRoute`, advertise `Capability::TlsTransform` from the guest bridge, widen the host TCP connector API to receive the full admitted TCP connect spec, and fail closed in the host authority when a flow requires TLS termination but the connector cannot provide it or when the guest marks the flow inconsistently. This lands the `C2` contract slice without pulling TLS runtime dependencies into `mvm-net` yet; the real terminator/re-origination path remains the next `C2` step. Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest,host,host-std,host-runner -- --test-threads=1` (82 tests); `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features guest,host,host-std,host-runner -- -D warnings`.
- [x] **C2b — Add the host TLS terminate/re-originate substrate for allowlisted hostname flows.**
      Add a feature-gated `mvm-net::host_tls` connector that terminates guest TLS under the per-VM name-constrained intermediate, re-originates upstream TLS with real certificate validation, and fits behind the existing `HostTcpConnector` drain/send seam so the guest still sees an ordinary byte stream. Thread `tls_intermediate` through `mvm-host-netd` config and select the TLS connector only when the backend staged per-VM material. Have the workload backend mint and persist that intermediate for hostname allowlists, encode the cert half onto the guest cmdline as `mvm.egress_ca=...`, and keep unrestricted/IP-only policies fail-closed because they cannot receive a name-constrained guest trust root. A follow-on compatibility slice keeps an opt-in `host-tls-boring` guest-facing backend available for targeted interop triage behind the same connector seam, but the default `rustls` guest-facing terminator is now the current proven public-path path again: the host connectors emit empty non-terminal host-to-guest `TcpData` chunks as pure ACK packets, and the rustls path now rewrites the initial plaintext server handshake flight into per-message TLS records so BusyBox-class TLS 1.2 clients continue past the first server flight on the HVF public path. The follow-on semantics slice is now landed too: transformed TLS routes with non-empty ALPN or plugin chains are denied at host admission time, the TLS connector keeps those paths fail-closed defensively, and guest fatal certificate alerts such as `unknown_ca` are surfaced as explicit transformed-certificate rejection details rather than an opaque handshake failure. Remaining `C2` work is plugin chaining over decrypted streams. Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd,host-tls-boring tls_connector_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`.
- [ ] **C3 — Add plugin-like transform pipeline.**
      Define plugin manifests, capability declarations, timeout budgets, max buffered bytes, stream-event APIs, and crash isolation. The first slices are now landed inside `mvm-net`: `stream_transform` defines built-in plugin manifests/capabilities/budgets, the guest/host handshake advertises `Capability::StreamTransforms`, guest synthetic-host `:443` opens now auto-carry a baseline `transform_chain=["audit","metadata-endpoint-deny"]`, `secret-replacement` now rewrites guest-managed placeholders into host-resolved secret values only for the bound target host, `metadata-endpoint-deny` now blocks proxy-style request heads that target metadata/control-plane endpoints, `response-leak-guard` reuses host-side ingress redaction to mask known secret values out of upstream plaintext before delivery to the guest, the backend now derives `stream_transforms` config from admitted plan secrets so transformed `:443` flows automatically append `secret-replacement` plus `response-leak-guard` when host-resolved secret material is present for both env-mounted and file-mounted secrets, the buffering-aware follow-ons are now in place for both managed-secret placeholder replacement and metadata request-head denial across split guest plaintext chunks plus split-secret leak-guard redaction across adjacent upstream chunks, metadata-endpoint denial now normalizes LF-only request heads, folded `Host` header values, inet-aton-style IPv4 authorities, IPv4-mapped IPv6 authorities, zone-scoped IPv6 authorities, and unbracketed IPv6-with-port authority aliases inside the same bounded request-head parser, built-in plugin panics/timeouts/buffer-budget overruns now fail closed during initialization plus every current execution seam (`process()`, `finish()`, and `finish_direction()`) instead of unwinding, stalling, or silently over-buffering the host authority, and `mvmctl run --dry-run`, `mvmctl machine start --dry-run`, `mvmctl machine inspect`, plus the human `mvmctl machine ls` table now surface whether the requested `:443` policy yields hostname-bound transparent TLS, raw-IP fail-closed behavior, or no transform path at all. Remaining `C3` work is broader HTTP semantics beyond the current bounded request-head authority parser, broader plugin isolation breadth, and richer user-facing policy wiring beyond these first inspection/preflight/list posture slices.
- [x] **D1 — Add UDP and controlled ICMP echo.**
      DNS-adjacent UDP already flowed through the synthetic-host DNS path; the broader admitted UDP slice is now landed too. Under `host-std`, the host authority now forwards admitted guest UDP datagrams over connected host sockets, drains bounded host-to-guest UDP replies back through the runner loop, emits UDP audit events for deny/forward/return/failure, and the guest bridge now synthesizes those admitted return datagrams back into guest IPv4 UDP packets. Controlled ICMP echo remains explicit reachability/audit behavior without opening arbitrary raw sockets, and the gated `transparent_net_e2e` harness already carries live allow/no-network ICMP proof on the approved HVF path. Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.
- [x] **E1 — Wire `machine run --net` / `--allow-host` to `mvm-net` on HVF.**
      The HVF workload path now resolves admitted boots through the runner-backed backend, which spawns the guest bridge plus `mvm-host-netd`, threads the authority UDS onto guest vsock port `5254`, and keeps real egress behind that host authority. The remaining default-HVF legacy residue is removed too: the runner no longer models a separate raw-egress path or reaps the old substitution endpoint, and the checkpoint child-boot path now uses `from_workload_hypervisor(...)` so it cannot silently fall back to the raw `HvfBackend` selector. Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-backend backend:: -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-backend workload_runner::runner:: -- --test-threads=1`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo test -p mvm-cli boot_forked_child_uses_workload_backend_selector_for_hvf --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-backend --all-targets -- -D warnings`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo clippy -p mvm-cli --all-targets -- -D warnings`.
- [x] **E1a — Cut workload launches over to the runner-backed HVF selector.**
      Add workload-only backend selectors so admitted workload paths choose `HvfRunner` on macOS even though the public effective hypervisor string still resolves to `hvf`. Cover `machine run`, transient exec/session boot plus teardown, and persistent OCI machine start/stop helpers without globally rebinding lower-level non-workload HVF commands to the runner path. Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-backend backend:: -- --test-threads=1`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo test -p mvm-cli resolve_effective_hypervisor -- --test-threads=1`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo check -p mvm-backend -p mvm-cli --all-targets`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo clippy -p mvm-backend -p mvm-cli --all-targets -- -D warnings`.
- [x] **E2 — Prove the user-visible behavior.**
      The gated public-path suite is now green on the approved HVF path with the default rustls-only `host-netd` build: admitted HTTPS fetch succeeds through `mvm-guest-netd` plus `mvm-host-netd`, denied-host and no-network HTTPS both fail closed, admitted ICMP echo succeeds, and no-network ICMP fails closed. Validation: `cargo build -p mvm-net --bin mvm-host-netd --features host-netd`; `MVM_HOST_NETD_PATH=$PWD/target/debug/mvm-host-netd MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 MVM_TRANSPARENT_NET_SMOKE=1 MVM_TRANSPARENT_NET_IMAGE=docker.io/library/busybox:1.36.1 cargo test -p mvm-cli --test transparent_net_e2e -- --nocapture --test-threads=1`.
- [x] **E2a — Thread guest-side egress trust into transient wrappers and retarget the gated smoke to HTTPS.**
      Reuse one `mvm-cli` helper for the staged egress CA env so both transient exec/session wrappers and `vm invoke` point guest TLS clients at `/run/mvm/ca-bundle.crt` plus `/run/mvm/egress-ca.crt` whenever the backend persisted `egress-intermediate.json`. Retarget the gated `transparent_net_e2e` defaults from `http://example.com:80` to `https://example.com:443` so the live acceptance path exercises certificate trust rather than only DNS plus TCP. The live green proof remains open under `E2`; this slice only makes the public path HTTPS-ready by default. Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-cli egress_ca_env --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-cli build_guest_wrapper_for_vm_prepends_egress_ca_env_when_present --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo test -p mvm-cli --test transparent_net_e2e -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-cli --all-targets -- -D warnings`.
- [x] **E2b — Add gated denied-host and no-network public-path acceptance cases.**
      Extend `transparent_net_e2e` with two more live-gated public-path probes: one where `machine run --image ...` receives no network grant and an HTTPS fetch must fail, and one where `--allow-host <allowed>:443` is present but the guest fetches a different HTTPS hostname and must still fail. Keep the probe logic self-checking inside the guest so a successful denial still yields exit 0 plus a sentinel line, and reuse one harness runner for cleanup/timeouts/log capture. This widens the user-visible `E2` proof surface around policy enforcement without depending on the still-open ICMP work. Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo test -p mvm-cli --test transparent_net_e2e -- --test-threads=1`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo clippy -p mvm-cli --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 live proof update

### 2026-07-09 admitted UDP slice update

- `mvm-net` now treats admitted non-DNS UDP as a first-class transport instead of an unconditional protocol failure. The guest translator keeps mapping UDP 4-tuples to stable flow IDs, the host authority now forwards admitted UDP datagrams through connected host sockets under `host-std`, and the runner drains host-to-guest UDP replies back over the same wire loop used for TCP.
- The guest bridge now synthesizes those admitted UDP reply datagrams back into guest IPv4 packets, while denial and failure `UdpDelivery` statuses remain safe no-packet outcomes rather than panics.
- Audit coverage widened with UDP deny/forward/return/failure events, and focused loopback coverage now proves both the host-authority seam and the guest↔authority UDP roundtrip.
- Validation: `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 C3 audit plugin slice update

- `mvm-net::stream_transform` now defines the first built-in decrypted-stream plugin contract: manifests, capability declarations, timeout budgets, max-buffered-byte limits, and directional plaintext event hooks all stay in the single crate.
- The first built-in plugin is `audit`. It passes plaintext through unchanged, counts guest-to-upstream and upstream-to-guest bytes, and emits safe per-chunk plus close-summary detail such as `plugin=audit ...` without logging plaintext.
- The host-side TLS terminator now executes admitted `transform_chain=["audit"]` routes over decrypted streams, advertises `Capability::StreamTransforms`, and still fails closed on unknown plugins before socket open.
- Validation: `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd,host-mvm-core stream_transform:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd,host-mvm-core transformed_tls_route_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd,host-mvm-core tls_connector_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd,host-mvm-core -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd,host-mvm-core -- -D warnings`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd,host-tls-boring tls_connector_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`.

### 2026-07-09 C3 secret replacement slice update

- `secret-replacement` is the first built-in guest-to-upstream mutating plugin for decrypted TLS flows. It reuses `mvm-core::policy::secret_binding::SecretBinding` plus the existing host-side destination-binding decision helper, so placeholder substitution and host scoping stay aligned with the broader secret-substitution model instead of forking a second policy shape inside `mvm-net`.
- The TLS terminator now constructs the transform chain with the admitted target host, denies `secret-replacement` before socket open when no binding matches that host, rewrites only bound managed placeholders such as `mvm-managed:OPENAI_API_KEY`, and fails closed if any unresolved managed placeholder survives in guest plaintext.
- The slice now buffers only the unresolved managed-placeholder suffix instead of the whole plaintext chunk. Safe plaintext before a split placeholder is streamed onward immediately, known placeholders can complete across guest chunk boundaries, and EOF with an incomplete placeholder now fails closed through the TLS terminator's finish path instead of silently dropping the buffered tail.
- Validation: `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 C3 metadata-endpoint denial slice update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` is the first built-in guest-to-upstream denial guard for decrypted TLS flows. It inspects only the HTTP request head on guest plaintext, so it can fail closed on explicit metadata/control-plane targets without introducing a general HTTP parser dependency or a second policy crate.
- The first slice catches proxy-style absolute-form request targets, `CONNECT` authority targets, and `Host` headers. It denies known metadata hostnames such as `metadata.google.internal` plus IP literals that fall into `mvm-core`'s mandatory-deny ranges, so proxy-style requests toward IMDS, link-local control-plane, loopback, and similar endpoints do not ride out through an otherwise admitted transformed TLS flow.
- The slice is still intentionally bounded to the request head only, but it now buffers split HTTP/1 request heads up to a fixed inspection budget so `Host` headers and `CONNECT` / absolute-form targets can be denied even when the guest plaintext arrives across multiple chunks. Deeper HTTP parsing and richer policy wiring remain open.
- Validation: `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd transformed_tls_route_with_metadata_endpoint_deny_plugin_is_admitted --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_proxy_style_metadata_request --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 C3 buffered transform follow-on update

- `mvm-net::stream_transform` now has a real finish-time denial path instead of string-only close summaries. `StreamTransformChain::finish()` can fail closed, and `mvm-net::host_tls` now threads that error detail through both the ordinary close path and the guest-facing transformed-TLS shutdown path.
- `secret-replacement` now rewrites known placeholders across split guest plaintext chunks, preserves bounded buffering via `max_buffered_bytes`, and denies EOF with incomplete managed placeholders.
- `metadata-endpoint-deny` now buffers only candidate HTTP/1 request heads, reassembles split `Host` headers and `CONNECT` / absolute-form targets up to the configured request-head budget, and still passes obvious non-HTTP plaintext through without stalling the flow.
- Validation: `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_secret_replacement_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_close_surfaces_incomplete_split_secret_placeholder_detail --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_proxy_style_metadata_request --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 C3 metadata request-head normalization follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` now handles a wider slice of HTTP/1 request-head syntax without becoming a general HTTP parser. The bounded request-head scanner now recognizes LF-only head termination in addition to CRLF and keeps deny decisions scoped to the same inspection budget.
- Host-header parsing is now continuation-aware too: a continued `Host` value such as `Host:\n metadata.google.internal` is unfolded and inspected as one header value instead of being treated as an empty `Host` line plus ignored continuation bytes.
- This closes a real bypass class at the decrypted-TLS seam: metadata/control-plane requests remain fail-closed even when the guest uses LF-only line endings or a folded `Host` header rather than the simpler one-line CRLF form already covered by the earlier slice.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_lf_only_folded_host_header_metadata_request --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 C3 obfuscated IPv4 authority follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` now handles a wider slice of authority syntax without becoming a general URL parser. The bounded request-head parser now normalizes inet-aton-style IPv4 authorities in addition to canonical dotted-quad literals.
- The new normalization covers the load-bearing obfuscation cases for metadata/control-plane IPs: a single decimal-dword authority such as `http://2852039166/...` and non-decimal `Host` values such as `Host: 0xA9FEA9FE` are now converted to the same canonical `169.254.169.254` denial result instead of slipping past `IpAddr::parse`.
- This closes another real bypass class at the decrypted-TLS seam: metadata/control-plane requests remain fail-closed even when the guest uses non-canonical IPv4 spellings in an absolute-form request target or `Host` header rather than a plain dotted-quad IP literal.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_decimal_dword_metadata_target --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_hex_host_header_metadata_ip --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_decimal_dword_metadata_request --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 C3 IPv4-mapped IPv6 metadata authority follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` now normalizes IPv4-mapped IPv6 authorities before consulting the mandatory-deny IP classifier. That keeps the bounded request-head parser aligned with the repo's existing SSRF-guard posture instead of treating mapped-v6 spellings as a separate allow path.
- The new normalization covers a real authority-alias case for metadata/control-plane IPs: request targets and `Host` values such as `[::ffff:169.254.169.254]` are now reduced to the same canonical `169.254.169.254` denial result instead of being treated as a distinct IPv6 literal outside the current deny ranges.
- This closes another decrypted-TLS bypass class without introducing a general URL or HTTP parser: metadata/control-plane requests remain fail-closed even when the guest spells a blocked IPv4 destination through an IPv4-mapped IPv6 authority.

### 2026-07-09 C3 zone-scoped IPv6 metadata authority follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` now strips IPv6 zone identifiers before authority classification so scoped link-local addresses reuse the same mandatory-deny IP logic instead of becoming parse failures. That keeps the bounded request-head parser aligned with the real network meaning of scoped IPv6 authorities.

### 2026-07-10 C3 unbracketed IPv6 authority follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` now recognizes unbracketed IP-literal authorities when they still carry an unambiguous numeric `:port` suffix. The bounded request-head parser no longer assumes every multi-colon authority with a trailing port is opaque host text.
- The new normalization covers another real alias class for blocked control-plane targets: request targets and `Host` values such as `::ffff:169.254.169.254:80` are now reduced to the embedded blocked IP instead of slipping past classification because the trailing `:80` prevented `IpAddr::parse()` from succeeding.
- This closes another decrypted-TLS bypass class without introducing a general URL or HTTP parser: metadata/control-plane requests remain fail-closed even when the guest uses bracketless IPv6-style authority text with a numeric port suffix rather than the canonical bracketed literal form.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_unbracketed_ipv6_with_port_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_unbracketed_ipv6_metadata_request --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-11 C3 percent-encoded authority normalization follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` now normalizes percent-encoded authority hosts before hostname/IP classification instead of treating `%` as meaningful only for IPv6 zone identifiers. The bounded request-head parser now decodes valid `%HH` octets inside reg-name authorities and only strips `%...` as a zone id for IPv6-looking hosts.
- The new normalization covers another real alias class for blocked control-plane targets: request targets and `Host` values such as `metadata%2egoogle%2einternal` now reduce to the same canonical metadata hostname, and dotted-quad aliases encoded as `169%2e254%2e169%2e254` now flow through the existing IP deny path instead of bypassing hostname/IP checks as opaque host text.
- The same normalization now reaches encoded authority delimiters too: percent-decoding happens before userinfo/host/port splitting rather than only after host extraction, so authorities such as `metadata%2egoogle%2einternal%3a80` reduce to the same blocked host-plus-port shape instead of bypassing the deny path because the raw parser never saw a literal `:80`.
- The bounded absolute-form target parser now decodes the post-scheme remainder before it separates authority from path/query/fragment too, so aliases such as `http://169.254.169.254%2Flatest/meta-data/` reduce to the same blocked metadata IP instead of bypassing the deny path because the raw parser never saw a literal `/` after the authority.
- The absolute-form target parser now also treats `\` as a path delimiter for the bounded metadata/control-plane classification path, so aliases such as `http://169.254.169.254\latest/meta-data/` reduce to the same blocked metadata IP instead of leaving the backslash inside the authority candidate as opaque host text.
- The parser now distinguishes structural raw `@` from encoded `%40` too. Absolute-form remainder decoding preserves `%40` until after raw userinfo splitting, decoded host candidates that still contain `@` are treated as malformed, and those malformed authorities now deny fail-closed instead of silently normalizing into a different host shape.
- The same malformed-authority rule now lands on the `Host` header seam too. Percent-decoded host-header authorities that still contain `@` are treated as malformed and denied fail-closed instead of silently normalizing into a different authority through the host-header path while the request-target path was already rejecting them.
- This closes another decrypted-TLS bypass class without introducing a general URL or HTTP parser: metadata/control-plane requests remain fail-closed even when the guest percent-encodes hostname separators inside an absolute-form request target or `Host` header rather than sending the canonical plain-text authority.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_percent_encoded_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_backslash_path_delimiter_after_metadata_ip --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_encoded_userinfo_delimiter_in_request_target_authority --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_encoded_userinfo_delimiter_in_host_header_authority --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_percent_encoded_metadata_request --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_percent_encoded_metadata_request_with_port --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_percent_encoded_path_delimiter_metadata_request --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_backslash_path_delimiter_metadata_request --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_encoded_userinfo_delimiter_metadata_request --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_encoded_userinfo_delimiter_host_header --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-11 C3 malformed percent-encoding authority fail-closed follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer treats malformed `%` escapes inside bounded request-head authorities as just another opaque non-matching host string. Invalid percent-encoding in absolute-form request-target authorities now fails closed with an explicit malformed-authority denial.
- The same malformed-authority rule now lands on the `Host` header seam too. Invalid `%` escapes in host-header authorities now deny fail-closed instead of leaving a malformed alias outside the current metadata/control-plane parser path.
- This keeps the bounded parser aligned with the host-side security claim: transparent TLS termination still does authority inspection without adding a general URL parser, but malformed authority text is now rejected explicitly rather than silently passing as an unclassified host candidate.
- Validation: `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_invalid_percent_encoding_in_request_target_authority --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_invalid_percent_encoding_in_host_header_authority --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_invalid_percent_encoding_request_target --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_invalid_percent_encoding_host_header --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-11 C3 IPv6 zone-id authority percent-validation alignment follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer strips IPv6 zone-id text out of host-header authorities before validating percent-encoding. Host-header authorities such as `[fe80::1%eth0]` now deny fail-closed as malformed instead of silently treating the raw `%...` tail as a valid zone id.
- The bounded request-target path was already stricter because it percent-decodes the post-scheme remainder before authority splitting. This follow-on aligns the host-header seam with that same fail-closed posture, so raw RFC-6874-invalid zone text cannot behave differently depending on whether the guest targets metadata through the absolute-form request target or the `Host` header.
- The valid encoded case stays intact: bracketed authorities such as `[fe80::1%25eth0]` still normalize onto the same blocked link-local metadata IP path after percent-decoding, but raw unescaped zone-id text now denies explicitly instead of being stripped before validation.
- Validation: `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_unescaped_zone_id_in_request_target_authority --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_unescaped_zone_id_in_host_header_authority --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_unescaped_zone_id_request_target --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_unescaped_zone_id_host_header --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-11 C3 raw userinfo authority fail-closed follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer strips raw userinfo delimiters out of bounded authorities before host extraction. Request-target and `Host` header authorities containing a literal `@` now deny fail-closed as malformed instead of silently normalizing to the suffix after the delimiter.
- This aligns the raw and encoded cases. Encoded `%40` authorities were already denied after percent-decoding; the bounded parser now treats a literal `@` with the same security posture rather than letting raw userinfo syntax behave as a separate bypass class.
- The denial now lands at the shared authority parser seam before host/port normalization, so neither absolute-form request targets nor `Host` headers can hide a blocked metadata hostname behind a raw userinfo prefix such as `metadata.google.internal@api.example.com`.
- Validation: `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_raw_userinfo_delimiter_in_request_target_authority --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_raw_userinfo_delimiter_in_host_header_authority --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_raw_userinfo_delimiter_request_target --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_raw_userinfo_delimiter_host_header --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-11 C3 malformed bracketed authority fail-closed follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer treats a leading `[` as enough to normalize a bracketed authority. Request-target and `Host` header authorities that open a bracketed literal but never close it now deny fail-closed as malformed instead of silently treating the remainder as the host.
- The same bracket validator now also constrains the suffix after a valid `]`: the bounded parser accepts either nothing or a numeric `:port` suffix, and rejects both stray trailing text and an empty `:` port as malformed instead of dropping them during host extraction.
- This keeps the request-head parser bounded while closing another normalization gap: malformed bracketed metadata IP spellings such as `[169.254.169.254` no longer collapse onto the blocked authority path as if they were syntactically valid literals.
- Validation: `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_missing_closing_bracket_in_request_target_authority --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_missing_closing_bracket_in_host_header_authority --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_invalid_suffix_after_bracket_in_request_target_authority --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_invalid_suffix_after_bracket_in_host_header_authority --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_empty_port_after_bracket_in_request_target_authority --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_empty_port_after_bracket_in_host_header_authority --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-11 C3 malformed non-numeric authority port fail-closed follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer treats plain `host:abc` or `ip:abc` authority text as an opaque host string when the parser has already recognized the trailing segment as a would-be port. Request-target and `Host` header authorities with a non-numeric port suffix now deny fail-closed as malformed instead of bypassing hostname/IP classification.
- The fail-closed rule is intentionally scoped to the bounded authority shapes the parser already recognizes: ordinary hostname/IPv4 authorities and the existing unbracketed IP-literal-with-port path. This closes another normalization gap without promoting the parser into a general URI grammar.
- Validation: `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_non_numeric_port_in_request_target_authority --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_non_numeric_port_in_host_header_authority --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 C3 malformed whitespace-bearing authority fail-closed follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer treats authorities that still contain embedded ASCII whitespace after decoding and normalization as just another opaque host string. Request-target and `Host` header authorities such as `metadata.google.internal%20evil` or `metadata.google.internal evil` now deny fail-closed as malformed instead of bypassing hostname/IP classification.
- This closes another bounded request-head normalization gap without introducing a general URI parser: malformed authorities that visually contain a blocked metadata/control-plane host plus trailing whitespace-bearing junk no longer slip through the authority classifier as unmatched host text.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_whitespace_in_request_target_authority --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_whitespace_in_host_header_authority --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 C3 malformed absolute-form URI scheme fail-closed follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer treats any `target` containing `://` as a valid absolute-form request target. The bounded request-target parser now validates the URI scheme shape first, and malformed schemes such as `1http://...` deny fail-closed as malformed instead of falling through as a quasi-absolute authority.
- This closes another bounded request-head normalization gap without introducing a general URI parser: malformed absolute-form request targets no longer get partial authority parsing semantics when their scheme token is invalid.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_invalid_scheme_in_request_target_authority --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_whitespace_in_request_target_authority --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 C3 duplicate plugin-id fail-closed follow-on update

- `stream_transform` no longer accepts duplicate plugin IDs in the effective transform chain. The shared chain-validation seam now rejects duplicates before construction or preflight validation, so a caller cannot stack the same built-in plugin twice and silently change transform semantics, buffering, or audit detail.
- This closes a real plugin-isolation gap in the shared single-crate pipeline: duplicate user-supplied plugin IDs are now fail-closed at the same boundary that already enforces built-in config validity and maximum chain length, while config-driven built-ins still use the existing `push_plugin_once(...)` dedup path.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests::duplicate_plugin_ids_are_rejected_fail_closed --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests::validate_plugin_ids_rejects_duplicate_plugin_ids --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 C3 duplicate secret-replacement placeholder fail-closed follow-on update

- `stream_transform::SecretReplacementPlugin` no longer accepts multiple bindings that resolve to the same managed-secret placeholder for the active target host. If overlapping target-host patterns would make one transformed flow see two `mvm-managed:...` replacements for the same placeholder, plugin construction now denies fail-closed instead of silently relying on config order.
- This closes a real plugin-isolation/config-integrity gap in the shared single-crate pipeline: duplicate secret-binding names remain allowed across disjoint target hosts, but the target-selected transform seam now rejects ambiguous per-host placeholder resolution before any guest plaintext is rewritten or any upstream socket is opened.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests::secret_replacement_rejects_duplicate_placeholders_for_same_target_host --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests::secret_replacement_allows_duplicate_binding_names_for_disjoint_target_hosts --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests::tls_connector_rejects_duplicate_secret_replacement_placeholders_for_target --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 C3 target-aware admission validation for stream transforms follow-on update

- Host admission no longer validates transformed-TLS plugin chains in isolation from the connector's configured transform state. `HostTcpConnector::validate_stream_transforms(...)` now receives the active target host, and the TLS connector reuses the shared target-aware transform validation path so ambiguous target-selected plugin construction failures can deny before `open()`.
- This pulls a real fail-closed boundary earlier in the single-crate pipeline: overlapping `secret-replacement` bindings for `api.example.com`, the simpler “no binding exists for this target host” case, and the zero-config `secret-replacement` / `response-leak-guard` cases are now all rejected during `HostAuthority` admission instead of being deferred until the connector would otherwise start opening the flow, while simpler chain-only checks still work for non-TLS or config-free connectors.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::transformed_tls_route_with_target_ambiguous_secret_replacement_is_denied_at_admission --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::transformed_tls_route_with_no_bound_secret_for_target_is_denied_at_admission --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::transformed_tls_route_with_no_secret_replacement_config_is_denied_at_admission --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::transformed_tls_route_with_no_response_leak_guard_config_is_denied_at_admission --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests::secret_replacement_rejects_duplicate_placeholders_for_same_target_host --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 C3 secret-replacement-before-metadata admission ordering follow-on update

- The effective built-in transform chain no longer lets host-side placeholder substitution run after metadata/control-plane inspection. `StreamTransformConfig::effective_plugin_chain(...)` now forces `secret-replacement` ahead of `metadata-endpoint-deny` whenever both are present, including the baseline path where metadata inspection is explicit and secret replacement is auto-appended from host-owned config.
- This closes a real plugin-isolation seam in the shared single-crate pipeline: guest plaintext such as `Host: mvm-managed:OPENAI_API_KEY` can no longer substitute into `metadata.google.internal` after the metadata guard has already inspected the pre-substitution authority text.
- The same slice also tightens the config/admission boundary around target-bound plugins. `StreamTransformConfig::validate()` now covers host-owned structural config checks, while per-flow chain validation and construction still happen through the target-aware path, so host TLS and host-netd config builders no longer try to instantiate `secret-replacement` without target-host context just to validate config shape.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests::effective_plugin_chain_orders_secret_replacement_before_metadata_endpoint_deny --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests::secret_replacement_runs_before_metadata_endpoint_deny_for_placeholder_host_header --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests::tls_connector_auto_runs_secret_replacement_before_metadata_endpoint_deny --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests::tls_connector_auto_appends_secret_replacement_to_baseline_chain --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests::config_json_rejects_empty_secret_replacement_target_host --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 C3 audit-before-mutator canonical ordering follow-on update

- The effective built-in transform chain no longer lets explicit plugin ordering change what the built-in audit plugin observes. `StreamTransformConfig::effective_plugin_chain(...)` now canonicalizes `audit` to the front of the effective chain before the later built-in security ordering rules run.
- This closes a real plugin-isolation and evidence-integrity seam in the shared single-crate pipeline: if a caller explicitly requested `response-leak-guard` or `secret-replacement` before `audit`, the audit plugin could otherwise record post-mutation byte counts instead of the original plaintext presented at that seam. Audit now consistently observes pre-mutation guest-to-upstream and upstream-to-guest plaintext even when the requested plugin order was backwards.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests::effective_plugin_chain_orders_audit_before_other_plugins --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests::audit_runs_before_response_leak_guard_even_when_requested_after_it --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests::tls_connector_response_leak_guard_redacts_upstream_plaintext --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 C3 localhost and loopback host-header parity follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` now has explicit regression coverage for host-header control-plane aliases that were already enforced by the shared authority classifier but not directly pinned in the request-head or transformed-TLS tests. Direct `Host: localhost:8080` and `Host: 127.0.0.1:8080` requests now have focused coverage at the shared stream-transform seam, and the transformed-TLS connector now has direct parity coverage for the localhost host-header path too.
- This closes a real proof gap in the broader bounded request-head story: the request-target path already had localhost/control-plane coverage, but the host-header path did not. The shared classifier already denied those aliases; now the guest-facing host-header seam is pinned directly so a future parser refactor cannot silently regress only that branch.

### 2026-07-13 C3 missing-host authority fail-closed follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer treats malformed authorities that syntactically carry an authority slot but no host as absent. Absolute-form request targets such as `http:///latest/meta-data/` and `Host` header authorities such as `:443` now deny fail-closed as malformed instead of collapsing to `None` and bypassing authority classification.

### 2026-07-13 C3 control-character authority fail-closed follow-on update

### 2026-07-13 C3 bracketed-hostname authority fail-closed follow-on update

Absolute-form request targets and `Host` header authorities such as `[metadata.google.internal]` now deny fail-closed as malformed instead of normalizing the bracket contents as an ordinary hostname.

### 2026-07-13 C3 duplicate Host-header authority fail-closed follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer accepts bounded HTTP/1 request heads that carry more than one `Host` header. Requests such as `Host: api.example.com` followed by `Host: metadata.google.internal` now deny fail-closed as malformed instead of letting the later header continue through the bounded authority classifier.
- This closes another bounded request-head normalization gap without introducing a general HTTP parser: the shared request-head scanner now treats duplicate `Host` as malformed at the same boundary where it already unfolds folded headers and normalizes authority syntax, so transformed-TLS metadata admission no longer depends on which repeated `Host` line the guest chooses to send.

### 2026-07-13 C3 malformed request-line fail-closed follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer treats malformed HTTP/1 request lines as “not parseable, therefore not inspectable.” Request heads such as `GET http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/ HTTP/1.1 EXTRA` now deny fail-closed as malformed instead of bypassing request-target metadata classification entirely.
- This closes a broader HTTP-semantics gap than the recent authority normalizer fixes: the bounded request-head scanner now refuses malformed request-line shapes before any authority extraction, so transformed-TLS metadata denial no longer depends on a request line being exactly parseable before it can even attempt the blocked-endpoint check.

### 2026-07-13 C3 malformed header-line fail-closed follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer ignores malformed HTTP/1 header lines that do not contain a `:` delimiter. Request heads such as `Host metadata.google.internal` now deny fail-closed as malformed instead of bypassing host-header metadata classification through the old “skip unparseable header line” path.
- This closes another broader HTTP-semantics gap beyond authority normalization: once the bounded scanner has committed to parsing an HTTP/1 request head, it now requires structurally valid header lines before continuing authority inspection, so malformed header syntax no longer behaves like an implicit allow path.

### 2026-07-13 C3 orphaned header-continuation fail-closed follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer ignores folded-header continuation lines that appear before any preceding header field. Request heads such as a bare leading ` metadata.google.internal` line now deny fail-closed as malformed instead of bypassing host-header inspection through the old “skip orphaned continuation bytes” path.
- This closes another broader HTTP-semantics gap beyond authority normalization: once the bounded scanner has committed to HTTP/1 header parsing, continuation syntax now requires an active header field to extend, so malformed folded-header shapes no longer behave like an implicit allow path.

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer accepts malformed HTTP/1 header fields whose name is empty after trimming. Request heads such as `: metadata.google.internal` now deny fail-closed as malformed instead of being treated as an ignorable non-`Host` header line.
- This closes another broader HTTP-semantics gap beyond authority normalization: once the bounded scanner has committed to parsing a header field, it now requires a non-empty header name before continuing authority inspection, so malformed empty-name header syntax no longer behaves like an implicit allow path.

### 2026-07-13 C3 whitespace-bearing header-name fail-closed follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer accepts malformed HTTP/1 header fields whose name itself contains ASCII whitespace. Request heads such as `Host : metadata.google.internal` now deny fail-closed as malformed instead of normalizing onto the `Host` path after trimming.
- This closes another broader HTTP-semantics gap beyond authority normalization: once the bounded scanner has committed to parsing a header field, it now requires a token-shaped header name before continuing authority inspection, so whitespace-bearing header-name syntax no longer behaves like an implicit allow or ambiguous normalization path.

### 2026-07-13 C3 invalid-character header-name fail-closed follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer accepts malformed HTTP/1 header fields whose name contains non-token characters. Request heads such as `Ho(st: metadata.google.internal` now deny fail-closed as malformed instead of being treated as an ignorable non-`Host` field.
- This closes another broader HTTP-semantics gap beyond authority normalization: once the bounded scanner has committed to parsing a header field, it now requires an RFC-token-shaped header name before continuing authority inspection, so punctuation/control-shaped header-name syntax no longer behaves like an implicit allow path.

### 2026-07-13 C3 malformed HTTP/1 version fail-closed follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer accepts malformed HTTP/1 version tokens after it has already parsed a request-line shape. Request heads such as `GET / HTTP/1.1XYZ` now deny fail-closed as malformed instead of bypassing later host-header metadata inspection through the old `starts_with("HTTP/1.")` check.
- This closes another broader HTTP-semantics gap beyond authority normalization: once the bounded scanner has committed to an HTTP/1 request line, it now requires an exact supported HTTP/1 version token before continuing request-head inspection, so malformed version syntax no longer behaves like an implicit allow path.

### 2026-07-13 C3 non-UTF8 request-head fail-closed follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer gives up and allows the flow when a bounded HTTP/1 request head contains invalid UTF-8. Request heads such as `Host: metadata.\xffgoogle.internal` now deny fail-closed as malformed instead of bypassing the metadata guard through the old `from_utf8(...).ok()?` early return.
- This closes another broader HTTP-semantics gap beyond authority normalization: once the bounded scanner has committed to parsing a candidate HTTP/1 request head, invalid text encoding in that head now behaves like a fail-closed malformed request instead of an implicit allow path.

### 2026-07-13 C3 extension-method request-head detection follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer limits HTTP request-head detection to the small built-in method list used by the early buffering heuristic. Full request heads with extension methods such as `PROPFIND /computeMetadata/v1/ HTTP/1.1` now enter the same bounded HTTP/1 inspection path and still deny fail-closed on metadata `Host` targets instead of bypassing the guard through the old known-method-only candidate filter.
- This closes a broader HTTP-semantics gap than the recent malformed-field checks: the bounded scanner now recognizes generic token-shaped HTTP/1 request lines once the first line is present, so metadata inspection no longer depends on the request method being one of a hard-coded verb subset.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests::metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_extension_method_host_header_metadata_hostname --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests::tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_extension_method_host_header_metadata_hostname --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 C3 malformed HTTP method-token fail-closed follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer lets malformed HTTP/1 method tokens bypass inspection after the broader request-head candidate widening. Request heads such as `GE(T /computeMetadata/v1/ HTTP/1.1` now deny fail-closed as malformed instead of entering the generic request-line path and continuing toward host-header inspection as if the method token were acceptable.
- This closes the companion request-line validation gap introduced by the broader method detection fix: once the bounded scanner has recognized a generic HTTP/1 request-line shape, it now requires the method token itself to be RFC-token-shaped before continuing the metadata guard path.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests::metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_invalid_method_token --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests::tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_invalid_method_token --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 C3 split extension-method request-head buffering follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer drops partially received extension-method request lines out of the bounded HTTP candidate path before the version token arrives. Split request heads such as `PROPFIND /computeMetadata/v1/ HT` followed by `TP/1.1\r\nHost: metadata.google.internal\r\n\r\n` now stay buffered and still deny fail-closed on the metadata `Host` target instead of bypassing the guard through the old complete-three-token-only generic candidate check.
- This closes the split-chunk half of the broader extension-method bypass class: once the bounded scanner sees a token-shaped method plus target on an unterminated first line, it now keeps buffering that candidate request line until it can validate the completed HTTP/1 shape or reject it as malformed.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests::metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_split_extension_method_host_header_metadata_hostname --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests::tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_split_extension_method_host_header_metadata_hostname --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 C3 split extension-method prefix buffering follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer drops a partially received extension-method first-line when the guest has only sent the method token plus separating space so far. Split request heads such as `PROPFIND ` followed by `/computeMetadata/v1/ HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: metadata.google.internal\r\n\r\n` now stay buffered and still deny fail-closed on the metadata `Host` target instead of bypassing the guard through the old “method plus target token required before buffering” check.
- This closes the earliest split-chunk half of the broader extension-method bypass class: once the bounded scanner sees a token-shaped method followed by the request-line separator on an unterminated first line, it now keeps buffering that candidate request line until target/version bytes arrive or the line is rejected as malformed.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests::metadata_endpoint_deny_rejects_split_extension_method_prefix_host_header_metadata_hostname --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests::tls_connector_metadata_endpoint_deny_blocks_split_extension_method_prefix_host_header_metadata_hostname --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 C3 HTTP/2 prior-knowledge preface fail-closed follow-on update

- `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer lets an HTTP/2 prior-knowledge preface bypass the metadata guard simply because it is not an HTTP/1 request head. Guest plaintext starting with `PRI * HTTP/2.0\r\n\r\nSM\r\n\r\n` now denies fail-closed as unsupported for metadata inspection instead of passing through the old HTTP/1-only candidate filter.
- This closes a broader HTTP-semantics gap than the recent HTTP/1 request-line hardening: the bounded scanner now recognizes and blocks the canonical HTTP/2 cleartext preface explicitly when transformed-TLS metadata inspection cannot reason about the subsequent authority semantics.
