### 2026-07-11 F1 host ICMP error-detail hard-cap follow-on update

- Host-owned ICMP failure detail is no longer bounded only by whatever `ping` happens to emit. `mvm-net::host` now clamps normalized `ping` stdout/stderr detail to a fixed UTF-8-safe byte ceiling before it is stored in `IcmpEchoExecution` or surfaced through the authority/audit path.
- The cap applies to both main failure paths on the host side: normalized non-success `ping` output and local `Command::output()` failure strings. This closes another host-owned detail-memory seam without changing the guest-visible ICMP status model.
- Focused unit coverage now pins both behaviors directly: oversized ASCII detail truncates to the cap, and truncation preserves UTF-8 boundaries for multi-byte text instead of emitting invalid strings. The broader `host::tests::` slice stays green after updating the older rate-limit tests to use the now-hard-bounded request-rate window.
- Validation: `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd normalized_ping_detail_truncates_oversized_output --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd normalized_ping_detail_preserves_utf8_boundaries_when_truncating --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-11 F1 host-netd retained TLS detail-budget surfaced-config follow-on update

- `mvm-host-netd` no longer leaves the TLS connector's retained-detail count budget as an implicit derived value. `HostNetdConfig` now surfaces `max_retained_tls_flow_details` directly, validates it at config admission, threads it through JSON config load, and passes the admitted value into `HostTlsConnectorConfig` instead of silently coupling retained-detail count to `max_open_flows`.
- This closes a real surfaced-config gap on the transformed-TLS path: the connector already enforced a bounded retained-detail count, but the daemon could not express or reject that host-owned budget explicitly and always collapsed it to the open-flow cap, hiding the actual TLS detail-retention seam from config admission and operator-visible policy.
- Focused coverage proves all three seams: `HostNetdConfig::builder()` rejects zero and oversized retained-detail budgets directly, the JSON config loader rejects the same malformed payloads before daemon startup, and connector-threading tests now prove the configured retained-detail cap reaches the real TLS connector instead of being replaced with `max_open_flows`.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests::config_builder_rejects_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests::config_json_rejects_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests::host_netd_threads_configured_tls_flow_detail_budget_into_tls_connector --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 host authority audit-detail hard-cap follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host` now enforces a final host-owned audit-detail byte ceiling at the authority boundary itself instead of trusting every upstream connector path to clamp activity and error detail first. `HostAuthority::record()` now sanitizes oversized connector-supplied detail before it reaches the audit sink.
- The cap covers the host-owned audit variants that can accumulate connector detail: `TcpOpenFailed`, `TcpBytesForwarded`, `TcpForwardFailed`, `TcpBytesReturned`, `FlowClosed`, `UdpFailed`, and `IcmpFailed`. Truncation is UTF-8-safe, so multi-byte detail is clipped at a character boundary instead of producing invalid strings.
- Focused host tests now pin both classes of proof directly: oversized ASCII error/activity detail truncates exactly to the authority budget on forward-failure and return-byte audit events, and oversized multi-byte close detail stays valid UTF-8 after truncation on the flow-closed path.
- This same validation pass also closed a compile-light cfg mismatch in `stream_transform`: the response leak guard buffering budget constant is now available on the config-validation path even when `host-tls` is not enabled, so `--features host` builds no longer fail before the authority tests run.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host host::tests::tcp_forward_failure_audit_detail_is_clamped_to_host_audit_budget --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host host::tests::tcp_return_audit_detail_is_clamped_to_host_audit_budget --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host host::tests::flow_closed_audit_error_detail_is_clamped_to_host_audit_budget --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host host::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 TLS connector retained-detail boundary hard-cap follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host_tls` now clamps retained detail at the connector-local retention boundary itself instead of assuming every caller already truncated the string before enqueueing it. `TlsTerminatingTcpConnector::store_retained_detail()` now applies the configured TLS detail byte budget before the retained detail enters any pending activity/error/close map.
- This closes a real host-owned detail seam that remained below the new authority-level audit clamp: retained connector detail count was already bounded, but the byte ceiling was still enforced only by convention at each caller. The connector now defends its own retention boundary directly, so future call sites cannot silently bypass the per-flow TLS detail budget.
- Focused TLS-connector tests now pin both classes of proof directly: oversized ASCII retained error detail truncates exactly to the connector budget, and oversized multi-byte retained close detail truncates at a UTF-8 boundary instead of storing invalid strings.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests::tls_connector_retained_detail_boundary_clamps_oversized_ascii_detail --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests::tls_connector_retained_detail_boundary_preserves_utf8_when_truncating --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 stream-transform detail aggregation hard-cap follow-on update

- `mvm-net::stream_transform` now clamps aggregated plugin detail at the chain helper itself instead of letting multi-plugin detail accumulation grow without an explicit byte ceiling. `append_detail()` now enforces a fixed UTF-8-safe detail budget whenever plugin detail is merged during `process()`, `finish()`, or `finish_direction()`.
- This closes a lower-level host-owned detail seam beneath the TLS connector caps: plugin detail strings were individually safe and later retention surfaces were now bounded, but the chain-local aggregation helper still had no explicit budget and could grow by repeated concatenation before the transformed-TLS connector consumed the result.
- Focused tests now pin both classes of proof directly at the aggregation seam: oversized ASCII aggregated detail truncates exactly to the stream-transform detail budget, and oversized multi-byte aggregated detail truncates at a UTF-8 boundary instead of producing invalid strings.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests::appended_stream_transform_detail_is_clamped_to_budget --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests::appended_stream_transform_detail_preserves_utf8_when_truncating --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 direct host address-resolution budget hard-cap follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host` no longer lets direct host DNS resolution collect an unbounded `Vec<SocketAddr>` before the TCP or UDP transport paths start iterating candidate addresses. `collect_resolved_target_addrs()` now enforces a fixed hard cap before the resolved-address set is materialized.
- This closes a real host-owned memory seam in the compile-light transport path: unresolved direct targets previously delegated completely to `ToSocketAddrs` and then collected every candidate into memory at once for both direct TCP connect attempts and admitted UDP socket binding.
- Focused host-std tests now pin both sides of the boundary directly: a budget-sized candidate set is preserved unchanged, while one extra resolved address fails closed with an explicit invalid-data error instead of growing the host-owned candidate vector beyond the cap.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host,host-std host::tests::collect_resolved_target_addrs_rejects_oversized_resolution_result_set --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host,host-std host::tests::collect_resolved_target_addrs_accepts_budget_sized_resolution_result_set --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 TLS PEM certificate-collection budget hard-cap follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host_tls` no longer validates PEM certificate-count budgets first and then recollects the same bundle into a host-owned `Vec<CertificateDer>` later at TLS-config construction time. Bounded PEM collection now enforces the certificate-count cap while the vector is being built.
- The runtime host-owned trust path is covered directly: upstream root PEM bundles now fail closed during root-store construction once collection would exceed `MAX_UPSTREAM_ROOT_CERTIFICATES`, and guest intermediate PEM collection reuses the same bounded helper for runtime TLS config construction instead of relying only on earlier validation.
- Focused `host_tls` tests now pin both sides of the new boundary directly: a budget-sized PEM bundle is collected successfully, while an oversized bundle fails with the explicit certificate-budget error during collection instead of fully materializing the over-budget vector first.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests::collect_pem_certs_bounded_rejects_oversized_upstream_root_bundle_during_collection --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests::collect_pem_certs_bounded_accepts_budget_sized_upstream_root_bundle --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 ClientHello summary vector-allocation hard-cap follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host_tls` no longer allocates full guest-controlled cipher-suite and supported-group `Vec<u16>` lists just to build one bounded ClientHello summary string for audit/error detail. The summary path now retains only the first few values plus the total count needed for `+N more` formatting.
- The same summary seam no longer retains an arbitrarily long copied SNI string before later detail clamping. SNI text is now truncated at a fixed UTF-8-safe summary budget during parse, so host-owned summary state stays bounded before it reaches any later detail clamp.
- Focused `host_tls` tests now pin both behaviors directly: overlong parsed `u16` lists keep only the retained prefix while preserving the total-count summary, and overlong SNI text truncates to the configured summary byte budget instead of copying the entire server name into host-owned diagnostic state first.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests::parse_u16_list_summary_caps_retained_values_but_preserves_total_count --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests::parse_server_name_list_truncates_retained_sni_to_summary_budget --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 guest-facing TLS handshake-buffer reuse follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host_tls` no longer allocates an extra host-owned `Vec<u8>` on the common unchanged guest-facing server-flight path just to pass bytes from `guest_tls.take_tls_bytes()` into the existing queue. The server-flight rewrite helper now reuses the original owned buffer unless an actual plaintext server-handshake split is required.
- The compatibility rewrite behavior is preserved: when the initial server handshake flight really does contain multiple plaintext server-handshake messages in one TLS record, the helper still constructs the split-record output; it just avoids the extra full-buffer clone when the bytes are already in the right shape or when parsing falls back unchanged.
- Focused `host_tls` tests now pin both behaviors directly: unchanged input reuses the original buffer allocation, while a multi-message plaintext server flight still rewrites into multiple guest-facing handshake records.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests::split_guest_server_handshake_records_reuses_input_buffer_when_unchanged --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests::split_guest_server_handshake_records_rewrites_multi_message_server_flight --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 TLS record-summary vector-join elimination follow-on update

- The shared TLS record-summary helper used by both `mvm-net::host` and `mvm-net::host_tls` no longer allocates a `Vec<String>` and then joins it just to summarize at most four TLS records for audit/error detail. The helper now builds one `String` incrementally while preserving the exact output format and the existing four-record cap.
- This keeps the same control-plane behavior while shrinking host-owned transient diagnostic allocation on a shared hot path: ordinary multi-record handshake/alert summaries still format identically, unknown record types still short-circuit to the same single-record summary, and the cap on the number of summarized records is unchanged.
- Focused host tests now pin both sides directly: a mixed handshake-plus-alert sequence still formats to the exact expected summary string, and a longer sequence still reports only the first four records without needing the intermediate vector-plus-join path.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host,host-std host::tests::summarize_tls_records_formats_multiple_records_without_vector_join --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host,host-std host::tests::summarize_tls_records_limits_summary_to_first_four_records --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 transformed-flow borrowed-detail append follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host_tls` no longer clones plugin/detail strings on the live transformed-flow activity path just to append them into the bounded per-flow activity detail buffer. The guest-to-upstream, upstream-to-guest, and upstream-finish append paths now feed borrowed `&str` detail into the shared helper instead of allocating a transient owned copy first.
- The owned append behavior is still preserved where ownership is actually needed: close/error detail and other retained state continue to use the same bounded helper, but live activity-detail aggregation now avoids one extra host-owned `String` allocation on each successful detail-carrying transform event.
- Focused `host_tls` tests now pin the borrowed helper behavior directly: borrowed detail appends into existing bounded detail state correctly, and an over-budget borrowed append fails closed without mutating the already-retained detail string.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests::append_detail_str_appends_borrowed_detail_without_preclone --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests::append_detail_str_rejects_over_budget_borrowed_detail --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 ClientHello list-summary vector-join elimination follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host_tls` no longer allocates a `Vec<String>` and then joins it just to render the bounded ClientHello cipher-suite / supported-group summary string. The list-summary helper now builds one `String` incrementally while preserving the same `0x....` formatting and `+N more` suffix behavior.
- This keeps the same diagnostic output while trimming another host-owned transient allocation on the transformed-TLS summary path. The retained list-value cap and total-count summary behavior are unchanged; only the intermediate formatting allocation strategy is tighter.

### 2026-07-13 F1 host-TLS retained-detail collapse follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host_tls` no longer leaves retained activity detail orphaned when the authority consumes only a flow's retained error or close detail. - The same collapse now lands on the live flow-local detail path too: `TlsFlowState::take_detail()` merges any still-pending activity detail into the returned error/close detail before the flow is dropped, so transformed-TLS failure and shutdown audit detail stays attributable without leaking connector-local state for dead flows.
- This closes a real host-owned memory/detail lifecycle gap inside the single-crate design: a transformed flow that accumulated activity detail and then failed or closed before the authority separately drained that activity detail could previously leave stale retained-detail state behind even though the flow itself was gone.
- Focused coverage proves both seams that matter for this slice: a connector-level retained activity+error pair now collapses into one returned detail and fully clears the retained maps/queue, an existing retained-detail replacement regression stays green, and a live transformed-TLS flow-finalization denial still surfaces through the real connector error-detail path after the collapse.

- This fixes a real transformed-TLS lifecycle seam: the TLS connector can generate final close/finalization detail only during `close()`, so the old order could record an empty or stale `FlowClosed.error_detail` even though the connector produced bounded close-time detail once finalization actually ran.
- The authority-level regression coverage now proves every host-side TCP/TLS close seam that currently feeds `FlowClosed`: a recording connector that materializes its detail only during an explicit guest `CloseFlow` still yields that detail in the final audit event, the same close-time detail is preserved on the send-failure path where `TcpForwardFailed` is followed by terminal flow shutdown, idle-flow pruning before a later admission preserves it, idle drain-time close emission preserves it, and a connector-originated `HostTcpEvent::Close` drained through the host runner loop preserves it too. The existing host-audit detail-clamp regression remains green under the corrected ordering.

- `mvm-net::host_tls::RustlsGuestTlsServer` now retains one outbound buffer and uses `split_off(0)` after `write_tls(...)`, so the returned TLS flight still owns the current bytes while the server keeps an empty but already allocated outbound buffer for the next guest-facing handshake or alert flight.

### 2026-07-14 F1 host-TLS bounded initial buffer capacity planning follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host_tls` no longer seeds several transformed-TLS hot buffers from zero capacity even though the connector already carries explicit per-flow byte caps and a fixed TLS read chunk size.
- The rustls guest-facing TLS server now seeds its retained outbound flight buffer with a bounded initial capacity, the optional `host-tls-boring` compatibility shim now does the same for both inbound and outbound TLS byte buffers, and `TlsTerminatingTcpConnector::open_flow()` now seeds each flow's pending guest-facing and pending upstream-plaintext buffers with a bounded initial capacity derived from the configured cap and `TLS_READ_BUFFER_BYTES`.
- This tightens the first-burst transformed-TLS hot path without turning every open flow into an eager hard-cap allocation: common handshake and early plaintext bursts now avoid immediate grow-from-empty reallocations, while the per-flow seed stays clamped to the existing 8 KiB TLS read chunk or the smaller configured cap.

- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::accepted_capabilities_seeds_capacity_from_handshake_bounds --lib -- --exact --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::handshake_accepts_only_configured_guest_capabilities --lib -- --exact --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-14 F1 host ICMP round-trip decimal parser follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host::parse_ping_time_micros()` no longer routes admitted ICMP round-trip parsing through `f64` when the helper only accepts a small positive decimal millisecond token and always returns an integer microsecond count.
- The shared host-std parser now trims and strips the optional `ms` suffix once, parses the whole-millisecond portion as `u64`, folds up to three fractional digits directly into microseconds, and rounds from the fourth fractional digit without floating-point conversion. The helper still accepts the same `"time=0.042 ms"` / summary formats that the broader ping parser already recognizes.
- This tightens another compile-light host diagnostic seam without changing guest-visible ICMP behavior: admitted ping replies still surface the same rounded microsecond counts, but the parser now avoids float parsing and makes the decimal-to-microsecond rounding path explicit.

- `mvm-net::host_tls::bounded_utf8_prefix()` no longer re-runs UTF-8 decoding in a decrementing loop when the helper is already on the truncated SNI-summary path and only needs the longest valid prefix under the byte budget.

- This tightens another compile-light transformed-TLS summary helper without changing the ClientHello summary contract: over-budget SNI values still truncate at a UTF-8 boundary, fully invalid in-budget names still fail closed, and a truncated invalid leading byte still yields the same empty retained prefix as before.

### 2026-07-14 F1 host-TLS PEM collection marker-bounded preallocation follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host_tls::collect_pem_certs_bounded()` no longer seeds its retained certificate vector directly from the raw caller budget when the actual PEM bundle can reveal a much tighter upper bound on how many certificates are even present.
- The shared PEM collection helper now preallocates from `min(max_certificates, count_pem_certificate_markers(pem))`, preserving the same empty-bundle, parse-error, accepted-budget, and over-budget fail-closed behavior while avoiding pathological capacity requests on callers that intentionally pass an unbounded cap for test-only parsing helpers.
- This keeps the earlier budget-shaped preallocation win on the production paths, but removes a real regression on the shared transformed-TLS test surface: `pem_certs(...)` can still use an effectively unbounded logical budget without asking `Vec` to reserve `usize::MAX` upfront.

### 2026-07-14 F1 host-TLS owned-detail move-through follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host_tls::append_detail()` no longer downgrades owned `Option<String>` detail into a borrowed `&str` immediately when the destination slot is empty and the helper can just retain the owned detail verbatim.
- The shared transformed-TLS detail helper now still ignores empty detail, still enforces the same byte budget, and still delegates to the borrowed append path when the slot already contains text, but on the empty-slot path it moves the owned detail directly into place instead of cloning it back through `append_detail_str(...)`.
- This tightens another transformed-TLS retained-detail path without changing wording or clamp behavior: close-detail and merged live-detail assembly still produce the same strings, but the already-owned first detail no longer pays an unnecessary owned-to-borrowed-to-owned hop.

### 2026-07-14 F1 host-TLS bounded usize-decimal scratch follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host_tls::append_usize_decimal()` no longer allocates its stack digit scratch buffer from `usize::BITS` when the helper emits base-10 text and only needs `ilog10(usize::MAX) + 1` decimal digits at most.

### 2026-07-14 C3/F1 metadata-endpoint deny retained request-buffer reuse follow-on update

- `mvm-net::stream_transform::MetadataEndpointDenyPlugin` no longer drains its bounded pending guest request-head buffer with `mem::take(...)`, which discarded the retained `Vec<u8>` allocation every time the plugin passed non-HTTP plaintext through, emitted an admitted HTTP/1 request after head inspection, or flushed a safe partial request at EOF.
- The built-in `metadata-endpoint-deny` plugin now routes those three drain sites through `take_pending_guest_to_upstream()`, which hands the caller the current owned bytes while reseeding the plugin’s retained buffer with the same capacity for the next request-head inspection cycle.
- This tightens another still-open C3/F1 buffered-plugin lifecycle seam without changing policy behavior: the bounded request-head parser still denies the same metadata/control-plane targets and still passes the same admitted/plain binary bytes, but repeated request buffering no longer throws away its retained guest-to-upstream buffer on every successful flush.

### 2026-07-14 C3/F1 plugin retained-buffer initial capacity planning follow-on update

- The built-in `secret-replacement`, `response-leak-guard`, and `metadata-endpoint-deny` plugins no longer seed their retained plaintext buffers from `Vec::new()` when each constructor already has an explicit bounded buffering budget.
- Those plugin constructors now route retained buffer setup through `initial_plugin_buffer(max_buffered_bytes)`, so the guest-to-upstream substitution path, upstream-to-guest redaction path, and metadata request-head inspection path all begin with budget-shaped retained capacity instead of growing from zero on first use.
- This tightens another still-open C3/F1 buffered-plugin lifecycle seam without changing policy behavior: the transform pipeline keeps the same substitution, redaction, and denial semantics while making the retained-buffer contract explicit at plugin construction time inside the single `mvm-net` crate.

- Those hot rewrite/redaction paths now route output setup through `initial_plugin_output_buffer(buffer.len())`, so no-op substitution and no-op redaction start from the current buffered plaintext bound instead of growing from zero while preserving the same byte-for-byte output, hit counts, and partial-prefix buffering behavior.

- `BuiltinStreamTransformPlugin::buffered_bytes()` now delegates the `ResponseLeakGuard` arm to the plugin’s real retained plaintext length, and `ResponseLeakGuardPlugin` now exposes that retained length directly through `buffered_bytes()`.

### 2026-07-14 C3/F1 chain-process borrowed first-input follow-on update

- `StreamTransformChain::process()` no longer clones the caller’s plaintext slice into an owned `Vec<u8>` before the first built-in plugin has even run.
- The chain process path now carries an optional owned `current` buffer and feeds the first plugin directly from the original caller slice, only switching to owned bytes once a plugin has returned a transformed `StreamTransformChunk`.
- This tightens another still-open C3/F1 plugin-pipeline hot path without changing policy behavior: the same per-plugin transforms still see the same plaintext bytes in the same order, but the common first-plugin path no longer pays an unconditional up-front clone inside the single `mvm-net` crate.

### 2026-07-14 C3/F1 empty-chain passthrough regression fix follow-on update

- The previous chain-process borrowed-first-input change no longer leaves the empty-chain case returning an empty byte vector for non-empty plaintext.
- `StreamTransformChain::process()` now falls back to `bytes.to_vec()` when no plugin has produced an owned replacement buffer, so an empty plugin chain once again behaves as a pure passthrough for non-empty plaintext while still avoiding the unconditional up-front clone on the first-plugin path.
- This restores the original chain contract while keeping the new hot-path tightening: non-empty plaintext is preserved byte-for-byte when the transform chain is empty, and the first-plugin borrowed-input improvement remains intact inside the single `mvm-net` crate.

### 2026-07-14 C3/F1 response-leak-guard flow-finalization fail-closed follow-on update

- `response-leak-guard` no longer returns a flow-finalization summary while it still has retained upstream plaintext waiting for directional finalization.
- `ResponseLeakGuardPlugin::finish()` now fails closed with an explicit error if `pending_upstream_to_guest` is non-empty, and the built-in wrapper keeps surfacing that denial through the ordinary `StreamTransformChain::finish()` path.
- This closes a real pipeline correctness gap: callers must flush the upstream-to-guest directional tail through `finish_direction(UpstreamToGuest)` before flow finalization, instead of silently dropping retained upstream plaintext while still reporting a successful close summary inside the single `mvm-net` crate.

- Focused coverage now proves that if an upstream TLS flow only delivers a partial secret suffix, `TlsTerminatingTcpConnector::close()` preserves the same fail-closed denial detail instead of silently collapsing the retained-tail state during explicit connector shutdown.

- `mvm-net::stream_transform` now exposes a stable host-netd-gated identity digest for each built-in transform plugin manifest instead of leaving receipt/audit/plugin-identity work to invent ad hoc hashes later.
- `StreamTransformManifest::identity_digest_hex()` now hashes the built-in plugin id, ordered capability labels, timeout budget, and buffered-byte budget into a SHA-256 hex digest, and `StreamTransformChain::identity_digests()` now returns the effective chain's `(plugin_id, digest)` pairs in runtime order.

### 2026-07-09 C3 dry-run transparent TLS posture update

- `mvmctl run --dry-run` now surfaces the first explicit user-facing transparent-TLS policy signal instead of leaving the transformed `:443` path implicit behind the general network posture label.
- The new preflight field and human output distinguish the load-bearing cases operators need to reason about today: `hostname-443-transform` when the requested posture yields per-VM guest trust plus host-side TLS termination on admitted hostname `:443` flows, `ip-443-fail-closed` when the requested posture only allows raw IP `:443` entries that cannot receive a name-constrained trust root, `mixed:...` when both shapes are present, and `disabled:...` when no transformed `:443` path is available.
- This is intentionally the first user-facing wiring slice, not the last one: it makes the current policy consequences visible at dry-run time without expanding the signed receipt schema or adding host-runtime configuration knobs yet.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-core transparent_tls_posture_label_covers_policy_shapes --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo test -p mvm-cli dry_run_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo clippy -p mvm-core -p mvm-cli --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 C3 machine start dry-run transparent TLS posture follow-on update

- `mvmctl machine start --dry-run` now surfaces the same explicit transparent-TLS posture signal that `mvmctl run --dry-run` already emits, instead of leaving persistent-machine `:443` consequences implicit behind only the general network posture label.
- The new machine-start preflight field stays intentionally outside the signed receipt schema, so operators can see `hostname-443-transform`, `ip-443-fail-closed`, `mixed:...`, and `disabled:...` at dry-run time without widening the persisted attestation shape for live starts.
- This keeps the user-facing policy wiring consistent across transient and persistent workload entry points while preserving receipt-schema stability; richer runtime/operator controls remain open.

### 2026-07-09 C3 machine inspect transparent TLS posture follow-on update

- `mvmctl machine inspect` now surfaces the resolved network posture, transparent-TLS posture, and enforcement tier for a persisted machine spec instead of showing only the raw `net` / `allow-host` flags.
- The new inspection helper stays human-surface-only: the persisted `MachineSpec` JSON shape is unchanged, while the inspect output makes the current hostname-transform vs raw-IP-fail-closed `:443` consequence visible without booting the VM.
- This extends the user-facing policy wiring from dry-run entry points into the steady-state inspection path for persistent machines; broader operator/runtime controls remain open.

### 2026-07-09 C3 machine ls transparent TLS posture follow-on update

### 2026-07-13 C3 structured transparent TLS upstream trust label follow-on update

- The structured transparent-network policy wiring now exposes not just whether `:443` is transformed, but which host trust source transformed re-origination will use. `mvmctl run --json` and `mvmctl machine start --json` now carry a derived top-level `transparent_tls_upstream_trust`, while `mvmctl machine inspect --json` and `mvmctl machine ls --json` now carry the same label inside `network_summary`.
- The label is intentionally derived from the requested policy plus effective operator config rather than persisted into machine specs or signed receipts. Hostname-bound transformed `:443` policies report `native-roots` by default, switch to `pem-bundle` when `host_netd_upstream_roots_pem_file` (or its env override) is effective, and report `not-applicable` when the resolved policy has no hostname-bound transformed TLS lane.
- This extends the earlier user-facing transparent-TLS policy wiring from posture alone into inspectable trust provenance, so operators and tooling can tell whether transformed TLS is using ambient native roots or an explicit PEM bundle without reopening backend-generated host-netd config files or re-deriving effective config out of band.

### 2026-07-09 C3 baseline guest-route plugin chain update

- Guest synthetic-host `:443` opens no longer leave the decrypted-stream pipeline dormant by default. `mvm-net::guest_packet` now emits `TlsTransformRoute`s with `transform_chain=["audit","metadata-endpoint-deny"]` on the wire for the built-in transformed-TLS path.
- That keeps the baseline plugin contract explicit in the protocol instead of relying on host-local inference: the first live transformed HTTPS path now always gets safe byte-count audit detail and the metadata/control-plane denial guard as soon as the guest resolves an admitted hostname and opens `:443`.

### 2026-07-09 C3 response leak guard slice update

- `response-leak-guard` is the first mutating decrypted-stream plugin. It reuses `mvm-core::ingress_redaction` so the known-secret masking logic is shared with the existing host-side response-redaction seam instead of duplicated inside `mvm-net`.
- The TLS terminator now validates plugin-specific config before socket open, so a transformed route that asks for `response-leak-guard` without configured secret values is denied fail-closed.
- When configured, the plugin leaves guest-to-upstream plaintext untouched, redacts matching known secret values out of upstream-to-guest plaintext, emits safe `redacted_hits` activity detail, and preserves a close-summary total in flow-close detail.
- The later same-day follow-on closes the split-secret gap too: `response-leak-guard` now carries a bounded `max_secret_len - 1` upstream suffix across adjacent chunks, redacts known-secret matches that straddle chunk boundaries before any guest-visible bytes escape, flushes safe non-secret tail bytes on EOF through the real TLS connector close path, and rejects configured secret values that would exceed the plugin's buffering budget.
- Validation: `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd response_leak_guard_redacts_secret_split_across_chunks --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd response_leak_guard_finish_direction_flushes_split_nonsecret_tail --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_response_leak_guard_redacts_split_upstream_secret --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 C3 admitted secret-config wiring update

- Admitted plan secrets now preserve the guest mount shape plus `allowed_hosts`, so the transparent TLS path has authoritative non-secret metadata for plugin wiring instead of depending on local binding reconstruction.
- The backend now resolves host-side secret values from the admitted plan plus local secret store, writes `stream_transforms.secret_replacement_bindings` and `stream_transforms.response_leak_guard_secret_values` into `mvm-host-netd`'s JSON config, and reuses those values to auto-append `secret-replacement` plus `response-leak-guard` to transformed `:443` flows behind the baseline guest-route chain.
- Destination matching for transformed secret replacement now honors the same `*.` wildcard host semantics the SDK's `SecretRef.allowed_hosts` already uses, so admitted wildcard secret bindings stay consistent across the older keyholder path and the new transparent TLS path.
- File-mounted secrets now participate in transformed secret replacement too: the backend derives replacement bindings from the admitted guest file path, so a guest placeholder such as `mvm-managed:/run/mvm-secrets/openai` is rewritten on the same bound-host path as env-mounted placeholders. Leak-guard coverage remains shared across both mount shapes.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo check -p mvm-core -p mvm-cli -p mvm-backend -p mvm-hostd -p mvm-net --all-targets`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo test -p mvm-cli managed_secrets:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-backend mvm_net_spawn::tests:: -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-core egress_substitution:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-hostd keyholder::admission:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_auto_appends_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo clippy -p mvm-core -p mvm-cli -p mvm-backend -p mvm-hostd --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 rustls interop coverage update

- The default `host-netd` build now has explicit in-process coverage for fragmented guest TLS 1.2 flights on the terminate/re-originate path: `tls_connector_handles_fragmented_tls12_guest_handshake_and_data` drives the rustls guest-facing terminator with a fragmented ClientHello, fragmented post-handshake guest flights, and fragmented application data.
- Validation is green under the rustls-only build: `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_handles_fragmented_tls12_guest_handshake_and_data --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.
- Follow-on live debugging confirmed and fixed the remaining BusyBox/HVF seam. The host-side audit comparison showed that the working path emitted multiple guest-facing server handshake records while the rustls-only path coalesced the initial plaintext server flight. The landed fix now splits that pre-CCS plaintext server flight into per-message TLS records on the default rustls guest-facing path, and the full gated `transparent_net_e2e` suite is green live again without `host-tls-boring`.

### 2026-07-09 rustls guest-flight framing fix update

- The rustls-only `mvm-host-netd` build now rewrites the initial plaintext server handshake flight into per-message TLS records before delivery to the guest. This preserves the existing rustls terminate/re-originate path but matches the guest-visible framing that BusyBox-class TLS 1.2 clients accepted from the compatibility backend.
- New focused coverage proves both seams under the default build: `tls_connector_splits_initial_server_handshake_records_for_guest_compatibility` inspects the first guest-facing server flight, and `tls_connector_handles_fragmented_tls12_guest_handshake_and_data` keeps fragmented guest flights green.
- The full gated public-path suite is now green under the default rustls-only binary, so `host-tls-boring` returns to an optional compatibility backend rather than the fresh-proof path.
- Validation: `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_splits_initial_server_handshake_records_for_guest_compatibility --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_handles_fragmented_tls12_guest_handshake_and_data --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`; `cargo build -p mvm-net --bin mvm-host-netd --features host-netd`; `MVM_HOST_NETD_PATH=$PWD/target/debug/mvm-host-netd MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 MVM_TRANSPARENT_NET_SMOKE=1 MVM_TRANSPARENT_NET_IMAGE=docker.io/library/busybox:1.36.1 cargo test -p mvm-cli --test transparent_net_e2e -- --nocapture --test-threads=1`.
- [ ] **F1 — Harden for production.**
      The first host-side flow-limit slices are now landed: `mvm-net::host` enforces both a bounded per-VM open-flow cap across admitted TCP plus UDP flows and a bounded per-VM guest-request rate across DNS, TCP-open, UDP, and ICMP authority requests, with `mvm-host-netd` carrying both limits through its config surface. The bounded synthetic-DNS pool is now surfaced too: `HostAuthority` already failed closed once its per-VM synthetic DNS map filled, and `mvm-host-netd` now carries `synthetic_dns_base`, `max_synthetic_dns_entries`, and `dns_ttl_seconds` through its builder/JSON config instead of leaving the synthetic-DNS range and TTL as only hidden authority defaults. Host-side synthetic DNS entries are reclaimable now too: the authority prunes stale synthetic-name mappings before new DNS allocation using the same guest-visible TTL it already emits in DNS answers, so a long-lived VM can reuse expired synthetic slots instead of exhausting the per-VM pool forever after enough one-off names. The guest-side synthetic-host cache is bounded now too: `GuestPacketTranslatorConfig` carries `max_synthetic_hosts`, defaults it to the same 65,534-entry envelope as the host authority's default synthetic-DNS pool, and evicts the oldest current guest-side mapping once that cache would otherwise grow without limit. Repeated synthetic-host replacement no longer leaves stale FIFO references behind either, so one frequently remapped address cannot keep growing guest memory while the cache entry count stays flat. Guest-side admitted UDP flow state is reclaimable now too: `GuestPacketTranslator` keeps stable 4-tuple UDP flow IDs under ordinary reuse, but evicts the oldest UDP mapping under flow-budget pressure and reclaims terminal denied/failed UDP mappings when `UdpDelivery` says that flow is done, while retaining bounded tombstones so late authority replies for reclaimed flows drop safely instead of hard-failing the pump. Repeated UDP flow removal no longer leaves stale flow-order entries behind either, so create/remove churn cannot turn the guest-side eviction queue into append-only history while the live UDP maps stay bounded. Guest-side pending DNS and ICMP authority queries are reclaimable now too: `GuestPacketTranslatorConfig` carries a bounded non-zero `pending_query_timeout`, stale pending-query slots are pruned before new guest DNS/ICMP admission and before response synthesis, and dropped authority replies for expired guest queries no longer consume the shared pending-query budget forever. Guest-side closed TCP retransmit tombstones are bounded deterministically now too: recently closed flows are retained in explicit FIFO order for retransmit suppression, the oldest tombstone is evicted first once that bounded budget is exceeded, and repeated reopen/close cycles no longer leave stale tombstone refs behind in the helper queue. Host-side admitted UDP flow state is reclaimable now too: `HostAuthorityConfig` carries a bounded `udp_idle_timeout`, `mvm-host-netd` exposes that knob through both builder and JSON config seams, zero values are rejected at config-load time, expired UDP sockets are pruned before TCP or UDP open-flow admission checks, and idle UDP sockets are dropped during drain cadence so successful but abandoned upstream UDP sockets cannot occupy the shared per-VM flow budget forever. Host-side admitted TCP/TLS flow state is reclaimable now too: `HostAuthorityConfig` carries a bounded `tcp_idle_timeout`, `mvm-host-netd` exposes that knob through both builder and JSON config seams, zero values are rejected at config-load time, expired TCP flows are pruned before TCP or UDP open-flow admission checks, guest sends on aged-out flows now fail closed with `CloseFlow { HostClosed }` instead of a protocol error, and drain housekeeping emits host-close messages for idle flows so stale guest TCP/TLS state is reclaimed too. The host connector timeout posture is surfaced too: `mvm-host-netd` now carries optional TCP connect timeout, bounded TCP read timeout, bounded UDP read timeout, bounded TCP idle timeout, and bounded UDP idle timeout into the host-owned upstream transport paths instead of relying only on baked-in defaults. The upstream trust boundary is surfaced too: `mvm-host-netd` can now carry explicit upstream TLS roots through its config surface rather than always depending on the host-native verifier default, and explicit PEM bundles are now bounded by both byte size and certificate count before connector construction so one oversized host-owned trust payload cannot consume unbounded config memory or parse work. Built-in plugin panic and timeout isolation are now landed too: transform plugin panics are caught and over-budget execution is denied fail-closed during initialization as well as every current per-flow execution seam, built-in plugin `max_buffered_bytes` budgets are now enforced instead of remaining manifest-only metadata, transformed TLS plugin chains are now bounded before admission/connector construction instead of remaining as unbounded guest-supplied vectors, host-owned transform-config vectors are now bounded before validation/connector construction instead of remaining as unbounded JSON/config payloads, the optional `host-tls-boring` compatibility backend now shares the same guest-facing pending-byte cap on its internal TLS I/O buffers instead of leaving that path with uncapped inbound/outbound `Vec<u8>` growth, the connector's retained activity/error/close-detail maps are now capped to the same per-VM open-flow budget instead of remaining as unbounded host-owned terminal-detail state, repeated retained-detail replacements/consumption no longer leave stale queue references behind that could accumulate host memory under long-lived transformed flows, both concrete host TCP connectors now carry their own defensive open-flow cap instead of trusting the authority layer alone to bound connector-local flow maps, and the protocol handshake no longer leaves capability vectors bounded only indirectly by wire-frame size. Real transformed-TLS connector tests now prove initialization, panic, timeout, chain-length, transform-config, guest-facing buffer-budget, and buffer-budget denials surface correctly through both the live TLS-flow error/close plumbing and the upstream-EOF directional-finalization seam. The host-side connector stack now carries six connector-local memory/detail/flow caps: plain TCP open-flow count is bounded, transformed-TLS open-flow count is bounded, guest-facing pending TLS bytes are bounded, guest plaintext queued while the upstream rustls client handshake is still in flight is bounded, host-owned transformed-flow detail accumulation is bounded inside one flow, and connector-retained per-flow detail entries are bounded across flows instead of leaving append-only terminal state unbounded. The control-plane handshake shape is bounded too: `Hello` and `HelloAck` now reject capability lists longer than `MAX_ENDPOINT_CAPABILITIES`, `HostAuthorityConfig` deduplicates/bounds its configured capability list before it can be emitted back to the guest or written into handshake audit events, the protocol's long-lived string fields no longer rely only on the outer JSON frame cap, and TLS transform metadata vectors no longer rely only on later host/connector validation. Canonical host/DNS names, correlation/trace/span ids, policy digests, plugin ids, and ALPN values are now length-bounded at construction time, stored denial detail is clamped to a bounded UTF-8-safe maximum before it can live in flow-open failures, close reasons, or audit output, and `OpenTcp`/`TlsTransformRoute` now reject overlong ALPN lists and overlong transform chains at the protocol boundary before host admission or connector construction. The wire adapter validates outbound `NetMessage` values before serialization now too, so protocol-invalid internal frames are refused before any bytes are written instead of depending on the read side alone to reject them after JSON framing. `response-leak-guard` now also keeps split-secret redaction bounded across adjacent upstream chunks and flushes safe tail bytes on EOF through the live connector seam. The detached `cargo-fuzz` surface is now broader too: `mvm-net` fuzzes hostile guest IPv4 input through `GuestPacketTranslator`, hostile framed host/guest authority bytes through `LengthPrefixedJsonAuthority`, arbitrary guest-message sequences plus fake connector-event cadence through `HostAuthority`, arbitrary guest packet-read / authority-message cadence through `GuestPumpLoop` plus `GuestBridgePump`, arbitrary authority-wire read/write cadence through `run_host_authority_until_blocked()` plus `HostAuthority`, the `host_netd` launch-config / JSON-config / in-memory stdio seam through `launch_config_from_args_and_env()`, `config_from_json_str()`, and `run_host_netd_on_stdio_parts()`, and the built-in decrypted-stream plugin pipeline through `StreamTransformChain`. The nightly security workflow now time-boxes all of those current `mvm-net` fuzz targets instead of only the first guest-packet parser harness, while guest-side malformed regressions also pin unsupported IP version, invalid IPv4 header/total length, compressed DNS-name, invalid ICMP echo, invalid UDP length, and invalid TCP header length failures in unit tests. Transparent-network audit output is now chain-signed and verifiable too: `mvm-host-netd` can write a per-VM signed audit chain under the host signer key while still mirroring structured JSON events to stderr. The first compile-light benchmark surface is now landed too: feature-gated `mvm-net-bench` exercises the public guest DNS translation path, the authority DNS path, and the host-runner TCP stream path while reporting wall-clock latency, throughput, and process CPU time without introducing another crate. Remaining `F1` work is broader connector/flow memory caps beyond these first TLS backlog/detail/config/retained-detail/connector-flow/host-upstream-root-config-limits/host-synthetic-dns-lifecycle/guest-synthetic-host/guest-udp-lifecycle/guest-pending-query-lifecycle/host-udp-idle-lifecycle/host-tcp-idle-lifecycle slices.

### 2026-07-09 F1 host open-flow limit slice update

- `mvm-net::host` now has a real host-side per-VM open-flow cap instead of leaving admitted upstream flow state unbounded. `HostAuthorityConfig` carries `max_open_flows`, defaults it to a bounded non-zero limit, rejects zero at config-build time, and counts both admitted TCP plus admitted UDP flow state against that cap.
- New admitted TCP opens and new admitted UDP flow IDs now fail closed with `DenialReason::ResourceLimit` plus safe detail before the host authority opens another upstream socket. Existing admitted UDP flows can continue using their established flow ID without being tripped by the cap.
- `mvm-host-netd` now accepts `max_open_flows` in its config surface and threads that value into the authority config it builds for the selected TCP/TLS connector, so the limit is not just a unit-test-only default.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_authority_config_rejects_zero_open_flow_limit --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tcp_open_is_denied_when_host_open_flow_limit_is_reached --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd udp_datagram_is_denied_when_host_open_flow_limit_is_reached --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd config_builder_rejects_zero_open_flow_limit --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd config_json_rejects_zero_open_flow_limit --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

- `mvm-net::proto::Target` now exposes a borrowed `host_and_port()` view in addition to the existing consuming `into_host_and_port()` helper, so host-side callers can derive audit `(host, port)` data without cloning the full canonicalized `Target`.
- `mvm-net::host::HostAuthority` now uses that borrowed view on both the admitted TCP-open path and the admitted UDP-forward path. Those hot branches no longer clone the full `Target` just to build owned audit strings before admission-limit or connector calls, while the retained flow specs and guest-visible results stay unchanged.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo test -p mvm-net target_exposes_borrowed_and_owned_host_port_views --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::allowed_tcp_open_invokes_connector_after_policy_allows --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::udp_datagram_is_denied_when_host_open_flow_limit_is_reached --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 UDP drain target-clone tightening follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host::drain_udp_messages()` no longer clones each retained UDP-flow `Target` purely to recover audit host/port data on the return and failure seams. The host-owned UDP drain path now derives audit `(host, port)` from a borrowed target view and keeps only the single `Target` clone that the guest-visible returned `UdpDatagram` actually needs.
- This lands on both current drain outcomes: successful host-to-guest UDP returns now clone only the packet target that leaves the authority, and drain-time UDP failures now take audit host/port from the retained state before the flow is reclaimed instead of cloning the whole `Target` only to tear it back down into strings.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::allowed_udp_datagram_roundtrip_returns_host_response_bytes --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::udp_drain_failure_emits_failed_delivery_audit_and_reclaims_flow --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 ICMP failure classification lowercase-allocation tightening follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host::classify_failed_ping()` no longer allocates a full lowercase copy of raw `ping` output before it decides between `TimedOut` and `Unreachable`. Failed ICMP classification now scans the original detail with a bounded ASCII-case-insensitive matcher, so the host-owned diagnostic path stops mirroring arbitrarily long `ping` stderr/stdout into a second temporary string before the later detail clamp runs.
- The guest-visible classification behavior stays the same: mixed-case `request timeout`, mixed-case `100% packet loss`, and mixed-case `timed out` markers still classify as `IcmpEchoStatus::TimedOut`, while unrelated failure text continues to classify as `Unreachable`.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::failed_ping_output_classifies_timeout --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::failed_ping_output_classifies_timeout_case_insensitively_without_lowercasing --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 ICMP ping-output assembly single-buffer tightening follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host::ping_output_detail()` no longer builds the stdout diagnostic string first and then appends stderr onto that owned buffer. The host-owned ICMP detail path now takes lossy stdout/stderr views, pre-sizes one output buffer from their combined lengths, and writes both streams into that one buffer directly.
- Guest-visible ICMP failure detail stays the same: stdout plus stderr still arrive in the same concatenated order for later timeout classification and UTF-8-safe detail clamping, but the host no longer takes the extra “owned stdout first, then grow it again for stderr” path.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::ping_output_detail_combines_stdout_and_stderr_into_one_buffer --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::failed_ping_output_classifies_timeout_case_insensitively_without_lowercasing --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 ICMP detail normalization in-place trim tightening follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host::normalized_ping_detail()` no longer trims ICMP failure detail by allocating a fresh `trimmed.to_string()` even though it already owns the full `String`. The host-owned normalization path now trims leading and trailing whitespace in place before it runs the existing UTF-8-safe detail clamp.
- Guest-visible ICMP failure detail stays the same: empty-or-whitespace-only detail still drops out as `None`, surrounding whitespace is still removed, and oversized multi-byte detail still clamps on UTF-8 boundaries after normalization.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::normalized_ping_detail_trims_in_place_before_clamping --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::normalized_ping_detail_preserves_utf8_boundaries_when_truncating --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 admitted TCP open failure-host lazy allocation tightening follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host::handle_open_tcp()` no longer clones the owned audit host string into a `failure_host` temporary before every admitted connector open. The host-owned TCP open path now materializes that second host string only on the actual `tcp.open(...)` failure branch by borrowing from the retained `TcpConnectSpec`.
- Guest-visible behavior stays the same: admitted opens still emit `TcpOpenAllowed` before the connector call, connector failures still emit `TcpOpenFailed` with the same host/port/error detail, and successful opens avoid the extra failure-only host allocation on the hot path.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::allowed_tcp_open_invokes_connector_after_policy_allows --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::tcp_open_failure_records_allowed_then_failed_audit_events --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 allowed-DNS lazy answered-name materialization tightening follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host::allowed_dns_response()` no longer clones the canonical `DnsName` up front just to build one shared `audit_name` string before it knows whether the query will emit a `DnsAnswered` event at all. The host-owned DNS path now materializes one owned name for `DnsAllowed`, returns immediately for non-`A` queries, and only materializes the answered-name string on the `A` path after synthetic-address allocation succeeds.
- Guest-visible behavior stays the same: `A` queries still record both `DnsAllowed` and `DnsAnswered` with the same canonical name, while non-`A` queries still return `Ok` with no answers and now prove they only record the `DnsAllowed` audit event.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::allowed_dns_query_records_allowed_and_answered_audit_events --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::allowed_non_a_dns_query_records_only_allowed_audit_event --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 synthetic-DNS expiry prune address-only reclaim tightening follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host::SyntheticDnsMap::prune_expired()` no longer clones every expired `DnsName` into a temporary vector just to remove those entries from both maps. The host-owned synthetic-DNS lifecycle path now retains live `by_name` entries in place, collects only the expired IPv4 addresses, and removes the corresponding `by_address` entries afterward.
- Guest-visible behavior stays the same: expired synthetic names are still reclaimed before pool exhaustion, freed address holes are still reusable without collision, and the TTL-driven host-side lifecycle semantics remain unchanged while the prune path stops copying expired canonical names just for bookkeeping.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::expired_synthetic_dns_entry_is_pruned_before_pool_exhaustion --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::expired_synthetic_dns_hole_reuses_freed_address_without_collision --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 ICMP ping-target borrowed-host selection tightening follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host::execute_icmp_echo()` no longer allocates a `String` for the ping target on the common admitted-hostname path just to pass `request.host` into `ping_command()`. The host-owned ICMP target selection now returns `Cow<'_, str>`, borrowing the guest hostname when no upstream IP pin is present and allocating only for the pinned-IP literal path.
- Guest-visible behavior stays the same: unresolved hostnames still ping the canonical admitted hostname text, pinned ICMP routes still ping the resolved upstream IP literal, and the later command/error/detail handling path is unchanged.

### 2026-07-13 F1 TCP close-path option-to-vec collect tightening follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host::handle_tcp_data()` no longer turns `Option<NetMessage>` into `Vec<NetMessage>` through `into_iter().collect()` on the current-flow-idle close path and the send-failure close path. Those host-owned control branches now use one direct helper that returns either `Vec::new()` or `vec![message]` without the generic iterator/collect route.
- Guest-visible behavior stays the same: idle guest sends still surface `CloseFlow { HostClosed }`, send failures still close with the same reason mapping and preserved close-time detail, and only the host-owned control-path conversion is tightened.

### 2026-07-13 F1 idle-prune flow-id vector elimination follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host::HostAuthority` no longer allocates intermediate `Vec<FlowId>` collections just to reclaim expired idle TCP and UDP flows. The host-owned idle-prune paths now scan for one expired entry at a time, remove it immediately, and record the same close/failure audit without first staging every expired flow id in a temporary vector.
- The TCP close-detail path stays intact: `close_tcp_flow()` now delegates to a shared post-removal helper so idle-prune, idle-drain, and explicit close flows all preserve the same final close-time audit detail. The implementation intentionally stays MSRV-safe on Rust `1.85.0` and avoids `HashMap::extract_if`, which would otherwise trip `clippy::incompatible-msrv`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 explicit transformed-TLS upstream trust selection follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host_tls::HostTlsConnectorConfig` no longer silently defaults transformed TLS re-origination to the host platform trust store. Building a TLS-terminating connector now requires an explicit upstream trust choice, so the host-owned transformed-TLS path must say `HostTlsUpstreamRoots::Native` or provide a bounded PEM bundle instead of inheriting ambient OS trust implicitly.
- `mvm-host-netd` now mirrors that fail-closed behavior at the daemon config surface: if `tls_intermediate` is configured, `upstream_roots` must also be configured explicitly. Non-TLS host-daemon configs keep their existing behavior, but the transformed-TLS path no longer claims attestable trust while silently falling back to platform roots.
- The same validation pass also corrected one stale host-netd threading regression whose helper was using a DNS TTL above the current bounded maximum, so the `host_netd::tests::` suite now reflects the live synthetic-DNS config ceiling again.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd connector_rejects_missing_upstream_roots --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd config_builder_rejects_missing_upstream_roots_for_tls_termination --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo check -p mvm-net --features host-netd`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 backend-generated transformed-TLS trust mode explicitness follow-on update

- `mvm-backend::mvm_net_spawn` no longer leaves the backend-generated `mvm-host-netd.json` ambiguous about upstream trust mode when it stages transformed-TLS guest intermediate material. When `tls_intermediate` is present, the backend now writes `upstream_roots = { "type": "native" }` explicitly into the per-VM host-netd config instead of relying on omission to imply platform trust.
- This keeps the real workload path aligned with the new connector/daemon fail-closed rule: the transformed-TLS config that production spawn writes is now explicit about trust posture, while non-TLS host-netd configs still omit `upstream_roots` entirely because they never build the TLS connector.
- Focused production-path coverage now pins both shapes: the older IP-allowlist spawn capture still proves non-TLS configs omit both `tls_intermediate` and `upstream_roots`, and a hostname-allowlist spawn capture now proves the real stubbed authority path writes both `tls_intermediate` and explicit native `upstream_roots`.
- Validation: `cargo test -p mvm-backend mvm_net_spawn::tests::host_netd_config_json_emits_explicit_native_upstream_roots_with_tls_intermediate --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-backend mvm_net_spawn::tests::spawn_mvm_net_authority_writes_config_and_records_pid --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-backend mvm_net_spawn::tests::spawn_mvm_net_authority_writes_explicit_native_upstream_roots_for_hostname_allowlist --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-backend --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 inspectable transformed-TLS upstream trust override follow-on update

- `mvm-backend::mvm_net_spawn` now lets operators pin transformed-TLS upstream trust for the real workload path through inspectable operator config without widening the signed plan shape. The new `mvm-core::user_config::MvmConfig.host_netd_upstream_roots_pem_file` field persists the PEM-bundle path in `config.toml`; when set, the backend reads that file and emits `upstream_roots = { "type": "pem_bundle", "pem": ... }` into the generated per-VM `mvm-host-netd.json`; otherwise it keeps the explicit native-root default.
- `MVM_HOST_NETD_UPSTREAM_ROOTS_PEM_FILE` remains as the higher-precedence ad-hoc override, so one shell/session can stage alternate host trust without rewriting persisted operator config. The effective resolution now lives in `mvm-core::user_config`, keeping the backend compile-time-light and the trust selector inspectable through `mvmctl ops config show`.
- Focused production-path coverage now pins both inspectable and override seams directly: `mvm-core::user_config` round-trips the new config key and honors env override precedence, config generation still accepts the PEM-bundle enum shape, the backend helper reads the PEM bundle through user config when no env override is present, and the real stubbed authority spawn path still writes PEM-bundle trust config for a hostname-allowlist transformed-TLS VM.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo test -p mvm-core user_config::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-backend mvm_net_spawn::tests::host_netd_config_json_emits_explicit_native_upstream_roots_with_tls_intermediate --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-backend mvm_net_spawn::tests::host_netd_config_json_emits_pem_bundle_upstream_roots_with_tls_intermediate --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-backend mvm_net_spawn::tests::resolve_host_netd_upstream_roots_defaults_to_native --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-backend mvm_net_spawn::tests::resolve_host_netd_upstream_roots_reads_pem_bundle_file_from_env --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-backend mvm_net_spawn::tests::resolve_host_netd_upstream_roots_reads_pem_bundle_file_from_user_config --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-backend mvm_net_spawn::tests::spawn_mvm_net_authority_writes_explicit_native_upstream_roots_for_hostname_allowlist --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-backend mvm_net_spawn::tests::spawn_mvm_net_authority_writes_pem_bundle_upstream_roots_for_hostname_allowlist --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-core --all-targets -- -D warnings`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-backend --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 transformed-TLS drain queue vector-elimination follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host_tls::TlsTerminatingTcpConnector` no longer snapshots every live transformed flow id into a temporary `Vec<FlowId>` on each `drain_events()` pass just to walk the connector-local flow map. The connector now keeps a bounded round-robin `flow_order` queue alongside the existing bounded `flows` map and drains directly from that queue instead of materializing a full key snapshot every time.
- The same slice closes the stale-ref seam explicitly. Closing a transformed flow now removes any queued flow-order refs for that flow id, and the empty-flow fast path clears any leftover queue garbage, so connector-local drain bookkeeping cannot grow into stale append-only state across close/reopen churn or manual cleanup edges.
- Guest-visible behavior stays the same: live transformed flows still emit the same `HostTcpEvent::Data` / `Close` events and close-detail plumbing, but the connector drain cadence now avoids one host-owned temporary allocation proportional to the live transformed-flow count on every pass.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_close_removes_flow_order_refs_for_closed_flow --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_drain_events_prunes_stale_flow_order_refs --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_terminates_guest_tls_and_reoriginates_upstream_tls --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 host TCP/UDP drain queue vector-elimination follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host::StdTcpConnector` no longer snapshots live direct-TCP flow ids into a temporary `Vec<FlowId>` on each `drain_events()` pass. The connector now keeps a bounded round-robin `flow_order` queue, drains directly from that queue, removes queued refs on close, and clears queue garbage on the empty-flow fast path, so host-side direct-TCP drain cadence avoids that repeated host-owned key-vector allocation while keeping queue state bounded across close/reopen churn.
- `mvm-net::host::HostAuthority::drain_udp_messages()` no longer snapshots live UDP flow ids into a temporary `Vec<FlowId>` before polling host sockets. Host-owned UDP drain bookkeeping now uses the same bounded round-robin queue shape, skips stale refs safely, removes queued refs whenever a failed or idle flow is reclaimed, and clears queue garbage when no live UDP flows remain.
- Focused regressions pin the new cleanup seams directly: direct-TCP close now removes duplicate queued refs for the same flow id, direct-TCP drain prunes stale queued refs when no live stream remains, and host UDP drain clears stale queued refs when no admitted UDP flow state is left. Existing direct-TCP ACK, UDP idle-prune, and UDP drain-failure coverage stay green over the new queue bookkeeping.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd std_tcp_connector_close_removes_flow_order_refs_for_closed_flow --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd std_tcp_connector_drain_events_prunes_stale_flow_order_refs --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd drain_udp_messages_prunes_stale_udp_flow_order_refs --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd std_tcp_connector_emits_ack_event_after_guest_data --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd drain_messages_prunes_idle_udp_flows --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd udp_drain_failure_emits_failed_delivery_audit_and_reclaims_flow --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 host UDP reusable read-buffer follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host::StdUdpFlowState` now owns one reusable `read_buffer` sized to `MAX_STREAM_CHUNK_BYTES`, and `HostAuthority::drain_udp_messages()` reads host UDP replies into that retained storage instead of allocating a fresh `Vec<u8>` before every `recv()` poll.
- The guest-visible behavior stays the same: successful host UDP replies still clone only the returned payload bytes into the guest-facing `UdpDatagram`, while failed and idle flows continue to emit the same audit and delivery outcomes. The hot no-reply / timeout polling path is tighter now because it no longer allocates a full read buffer on every drain pass for every live UDP flow.
- Focused coverage extends the live UDP round-trip seam directly: after a successful host UDP reply, the admitted UDP flow stays open and still retains its full reusable read buffer for later drains. The stale-queue, idle-prune, and drain-failure regressions remain green across the new storage shape.

### 2026-07-13 F1 host direct-TCP reusable read-buffer follow-on update

The hot no-data polling path is tighter now because each live direct-TCP flow keeps one provisioned buffer instead of rebuilding one per drain pass.

The ACK-only, stale-queue cleanup, close cleanup, open-flow-limit, and clippy gates remain green across the new storage shape.

### 2026-07-13 F1 host resolved-address iterator follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host::connect_target_with_config()` and `bind_udp_target()` no longer force host-side direct TCP connect and UDP bind paths to materialize a temporary `Vec<SocketAddr>` of every resolved destination before they even begin trying sockets. Oversized resolution result sets still fail closed with the same `InvalidData` error text, but budget-sized or early-success resolutions no longer pay for an intermediate address vector before the first usable address is tried.

### 2026-07-14 F1 transformed-TLS bounded connector-state capacity follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host_tls::TlsTerminatingTcpConnector` no longer leaves its bounded connector-local flow and retained-detail state at zero initial capacity. The constructor now seeds the transformed-flow map, transformed-flow round-robin queue, retained activity/error/close maps, and retained-detail FIFO directly from the configured `max_open_flows` and `max_retained_tls_flow_details` caps.
- This keeps the host-owned transformed-TLS connector aligned with the same F1 posture already applied elsewhere in the single-crate networking stack: once the config says a connector can hold at most `N` live flows or retained details, its internal bounded storage now starts from that same known envelope instead of growing from repeated reallocation as the VM approaches steady-state load.
- Focused regressions pin both seams directly by asserting the connector seeds flow storage from `max_open_flows` and retained-detail storage from `max_retained_tls_flow_details`, while the broader `host-netd` library sweep continues to cover the existing transformed-TLS behavior.

- This keeps the two host-side TCP connector paths aligned structurally: direct TCP still emits the same ACK-only `HostTcpEvent::Data` after guest writes and before any later host reply, but the ACK event shape now lives in one helper rather than remaining duplicated inside the direct connector’s hot drain path.

### 2026-07-13 F1 shared host UDP terminal-bookkeeping follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host::HostAuthority` no longer repeats host-owned UDP terminal cleanup across idle-prune, admitted-send failure, and drain-failure branches. The host authority now shares one narrow set of helpers for removing retained UDP flow state and flow-order refs, recording bounded `UdpFailed` audit events from a `Target` plus upstream IP, and building the guest-visible failed-delivery message.
- Guest-visible behavior stays the same: idle UDP flows still emit no guest close/message, admitted-send failures still emit `UdpDelivery { Failed(...) }`, drain failures still emit the same failed delivery plus audit detail, and all three paths still reclaim retained UDP flow state. The change is structural: terminal UDP bookkeeping now moves through one shared host-owned path instead of three partially duplicated branches.
- Existing live-path coverage already pins the shared seam sufficiently: idle-prune remains green during `drain_messages()`, the connected-unused-port regression still proves failed delivery plus audit plus state reclamation, the live UDP round-trip remains green, and the flow-limit prune regression still proves expired UDP state is reclaimed before a new admitted UDP flow is considered.
