- `mvm-net::host_tls::TlsFlowState` no longer keeps the transformed-flow close-detail append logic open-coded at finalization time. The final `finish_stream_transforms()` path now shares one helper that appends close detail under the configured TLS flow-detail byte cap.
- Guest-visible behavior stays the same: transformed-flow close detail still accumulates in the same `close_detail` slot, and overflow still fails closed with the same retained transform error detail instead of silently truncating or dropping the finalization detail. The change is structural rather than semantic.
- Focused regression pins the shared seam directly: a new direct flow-state helper test proves the shared close-detail append helper still returns `TransportError::TransformError` on overflow and preserves the pre-existing retained close detail for the subsequent `take_detail()` drain.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_flow_state_close_detail_helper_preserves_overflow_error --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 shared transformed-flow pending-buffer length check follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host_tls::TlsFlowState` no longer computes checked pending-buffer lengths and fail-closed transform-error detail separately for the guest-facing TLS backlog path and the upstream plaintext backlog path. Those two buffering sites now share one helper that validates `current_len + added_len` against both arithmetic overflow and the configured backlog cap before any bytes are appended.
- Guest-visible behavior stays the same: the guest-facing TLS backlog path still surfaces the same host-accounting overflow detail and pending-byte-budget detail, and the upstream plaintext backlog path still surfaces its matching overflow and exceeded-budget detail strings before the byte queues are extended. The change is structural rather than semantic.
- Focused regressions pin the shared seam directly: new direct flow-state helper tests prove the shared length-check helper still returns `TransportError::TransformError` with the same exceeded-budget detail and the same arithmetic-overflow detail on the two fail-closed branches.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_flow_state_pending_buffer_len_helper_preserves_exceeded_budget_detail --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_flow_state_pending_buffer_len_helper_preserves_overflow_detail --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 shared transformed-flow guest close-notify path follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host_tls::TlsFlowState` no longer keeps the guest-facing TLS close-notify plus flush sequence open-coded in both the guest half-close path and the upstream-EOF close path. Those transformed-flow close sites now share one helper that sends guest close-notify and stages the resulting TLS bytes into the pending guest buffer.
- Guest-visible behavior stays the same: both transformed-flow close paths still emit the same guest-facing TLS close-notify bytes before continuing into upstream shutdown or finalization. The change is structural rather than semantic.
- Focused regression pins the shared seam directly: a new direct flow-state helper test proves the shared guest close-notify helper stages guest-visible TLS bytes into `pending_guest_bytes`.

### 2026-07-13 F1 shared transformed-flow guest chunk summary bookkeeping follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host_tls::TlsFlowState` no longer keeps non-empty guest chunk summary bookkeeping open-coded inside `ingest_guest_chunk()`. That path now shares one helper that refreshes `last_guest_record_summary` from the current guest TLS record bytes and captures the first successfully parsed ClientHello summary only once.
- Guest-visible behavior stays the same: every non-empty guest chunk still refreshes the retained last-record summary, while the first ClientHello summary remains sticky for later detail surfacing instead of being overwritten by subsequent guest chunks. The change is structural rather than semantic.

### 2026-07-13 F1 shared transformed-flow pending-guest-event ordering follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host_tls::TlsFlowState::drain_event()` no longer keeps the same pending-guest-event-before-host-close ordering open-coded around both the pre-read and post-read drain steps. Those transformed-flow drain sites now share one helper that returns a queued guest event first and only falls back to a terminal `HostClosed` event when no guest output is pending.
- Guest-visible behavior stays the same: a queued ACK or guest payload still wins before terminal host-close emission, while `close_pending` remains set so the eventual `HostClosed` event can still drain after the queued guest output is consumed. The change is structural rather than semantic.

### 2026-07-13 F1 shared transformed-flow guest plaintext write boundary follow-on update

- `mvm-net::host_tls::TlsFlowState` no longer keeps the same guest-facing TLS plaintext write boundary open-coded in both the live upstream plaintext path and the upstream-finish path. Those transformed-flow upstream-to-guest sites now share one helper that writes plaintext through the guest TLS terminator and preserves the existing transform-error mapping on failure.
- Guest-visible behavior stays the same: live upstream plaintext and finish-direction bytes still enter the same guest TLS terminator, and the finish-direction path still flushes staged TLS bytes into the pending guest buffer after the shared write helper succeeds. The change is structural rather than semantic.

### 2026-07-09 C3/F1 built-in plugin panic isolation update

- `mvm-net::stream_transform` now wraps built-in plugin `process()` and `finish()` execution in panic isolation, so a buggy transform plugin cannot unwind through `mvm-host-netd` and take out the whole authority loop.
- The fail-closed behavior is explicit: a caught panic becomes `StreamTransformError::PluginDenied` with safe detail naming the phase (`plaintext transform` or `flow finalization`) instead of an unstructured process crash.
- Focused coverage uses test-only panic plugins to prove both seams: a panic during plaintext processing and a panic during close/finalization are both caught and turned into flow denials, while the rest of the stream-transform suite remains green.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd panic_in_plugin_process_is_caught_and_denied_fail_closed --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd panic_in_plugin_finish_is_caught_and_denied_fail_closed --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 C3/F1 built-in plugin timeout isolation update

- `mvm-net::stream_transform` now enforces the built-in plugin timeout budgets that were previously only manifest metadata. Each built-in plugin `process()` and `finish()` phase is timed, and an over-budget execution fails closed instead of silently running long in the host authority loop.
- The fail-closed behavior is explicit: an over-budget plugin becomes `StreamTransformError::PluginDenied` with safe detail naming the phase (`plaintext transform` or `flow finalization`) so the close reason remains attributable without exposing plaintext.
- Focused coverage uses test-only slow plugins with a tiny timeout budget to prove both seams: an over-budget plaintext transform and an over-budget finalization both convert into flow denials, while the broader stream-transform suite remains green.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd timeout_in_plugin_process_is_caught_and_denied_fail_closed --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd timeout_in_plugin_finish_is_caught_and_denied_fail_closed --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 C3/F1 built-in plugin initialization isolation follow-on update

- `mvm-net::stream_transform` now wraps built-in plugin construction in the same fail-closed isolation posture as the live plaintext phases, so a buggy plugin cannot panic or stall the authority during chain creation before any bytes flow.
- Constructor-time panics now surface as `PluginDenied` detail naming the `initialization` phase, and constructor-time timeout overruns are denied against the plugin timeout budget instead of being deferred into later socket or TLS handling.
- The real transformed-TLS connector now classifies stream-transform construction failures at `open()` time as `TransportError::TransformError` with the stored denial detail, rather than surfacing them as generic TLS handshake failures. The fail-early validation seam is tighter too: `validate_stream_transforms()` now rejects response-leak-guard secret values that would exceed the buffering budget instead of letting that config slip through until live flow open.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd construction_is_caught_and_denied_fail_closed --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd initialization --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd response_leak_guard_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 C3/F1 built-in plugin directional-finalization isolation follow-on update

- `mvm-net::stream_transform` no longer leaves `finish_direction()` outside the built-in plugin fail-closed isolation boundary. Directional finalization now shares the same panic, timeout-budget, and buffered-byte enforcement wrapper as `process()` and `finish()`.
- The real transformed-TLS connector now proves that upstream-EOF finalization goes through that same guard. When `TlsFlowState::flush_upstream_finish_bytes()` runs during guest-facing shutdown, a buggy or over-budget plugin now surfaces as `TransportError::TransformError` with stored denial detail instead of unwinding, stalling, or silently over-buffering the host authority.
- Focused unit coverage now proves panic, timeout, and buffer-budget denial at the `finish_direction()` seam itself, and focused connector coverage now proves the same three denials survive the real live EOF path through `host_tls`.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd finish_direction --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd directional_finalization --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 F1 host-netd synthetic DNS cap wiring follow-on update

- `HostAuthority` already enforced a bounded per-VM synthetic DNS pool, but `mvm-host-netd` had left that cap as only an internal default. `HostNetdConfig` and the JSON config surface now expose `max_synthetic_dns_entries`, so operators can set the bound explicitly per host-netd instance.
- Builder-time and JSON-config validation now reject zero synthetic-DNS limits through the same authority-config validation path used by the core host authority, so host-netd cannot silently load an invalid cap that the runtime would later have to correct.
- `authority_config_for_tcp_connector()` now threads the configured synthetic-DNS cap into the real `HostAuthorityConfig` that powers the selected TCP/TLS connector, so the surfaced limit is not documentation-only or test-only wiring.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 F1 host-netd upstream TLS roots wiring follow-on update

- The TLS connector already supported both native trust and a pinned PEM root bundle, but `mvm-host-netd` had not exposed that choice through its own config surface. `HostNetdConfig` and the JSON config file now carry `upstream_roots`, so host-netd can use either the native verifier or an explicit PEM bundle without code changes.
- Validation is fail-closed at config load time for the public config seam: empty PEM bundles are rejected before the daemon starts, and a focused wiring test proves the chosen root policy is threaded into the real `TlsTerminatingTcpConnector` config rather than being dropped on the floor.
- This keeps the implementation single-crate and compile-light while tightening the attestable trust story for transparent TLS egress: the host-side outbound trust source is now an explicit config choice instead of only an implicit platform default.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 F1 host-netd synthetic DNS base + TTL wiring follow-on update

- The host authority already carried a synthetic DNS base address and DNS TTL, but `mvm-host-netd` had still left both as hidden defaults. `HostNetdConfig` and the JSON config surface now carry `synthetic_dns_base` and `dns_ttl_seconds`, so host-netd can explicitly shape the synthetic-DNS range and the guest-visible TTL without code changes.
- Validation stays fail-closed through the shared authority-config path: zero TTL values are rejected before startup, and out-of-range synthetic-DNS base-plus-pool combinations fail config load instead of producing a truncated or wrapping address range at runtime.
- A focused wiring test now proves host-netd threads the configured synthetic-DNS base, entry cap, and TTL into the real `HostAuthorityConfig`, so the surfaced DNS settings drive the actual guest-visible synthetic DNS behavior rather than remaining documentation-only knobs.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 F1 host-netd TCP connector timeout wiring follow-on update

- The plain upstream TCP connector and the TLS re-origination connector already supported host-side timeout config, but `mvm-host-netd` had left both stuck behind defaults. `HostNetdConfig` and the JSON config surface now expose optional `tcp_connect_timeout` plus bounded `tcp_read_timeout`, and host-netd threads them into both connector paths.
- Validation is fail-closed at config load time: zero connect timeout and zero read timeout values are rejected before startup rather than being deferred into platform socket behavior, and focused wiring tests prove the selected timeouts reach both the plain `StdTcpConnector` and the `TlsTerminatingTcpConnector`.
- This keeps the work single-crate and compile-light while tightening the host-owned transport posture: the daemon's upstream timeout behavior is now explicit, testable, and operator-configurable instead of only implicit in code defaults.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 F1 host authority UDP read-timeout wiring follow-on update

- The host authority's UDP return path had still been pinned to the plain TCP connector's default read timeout. `HostAuthorityConfig` now carries a real `udp_read_timeout`, and `mvm-host-netd` now exposes the same bounded `udp_read_timeout` knob through its builder and JSON config surfaces.
- Validation is fail-closed at both seams: the authority config rejects zero UDP read timeout values, host-netd rejects zero `udp_read_timeout_millis` at config load time, and a focused wiring test proves host-netd threads the selected timeout into the real `HostAuthorityConfig` that governs UDP flow state.
- This keeps the work single-crate and compile-light while removing one of the remaining hidden host-owned transport defaults: the UDP return-path socket posture is now explicit and operator-configurable instead of silently inheriting an unrelated TCP default.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_authority_config_rejects_zero_udp_read_timeout --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 F1 compile-light latency/throughput/CPU benchmark surface update

- `mvm-net` now ships a feature-gated in-crate benchmark binary instead of leaving the explicit performance-proof bucket entirely open. `crates/mvm-net/src/bin/mvm-net-bench.rs` runs three public-API scenarios: guest DNS translate-plus-synthesize round trips through `GuestPacketTranslator`, host authority DNS handling through `HostAuthority`, and steady-state guest-to-host TCP streaming through `run_host_authority_until_blocked()`.
- The benchmark path stays compile-light and single-crate. A new `bench` feature gates the binary plus the tiny safe `cpu-time` dependency, so ordinary `mvm-net` builds do not pick up a benchmark framework or a second benchmark crate. The harness reports per-scenario wall-clock latency, throughput where bytes move, and process CPU time so operators can compare latency/throughput tradeoffs without live VM boot overhead.
- Focused coverage pins the new surface itself: the benchmark argument parser rejects zero-valued knobs, accepts explicit overrides, and the helper metric calculations stay stable. A sample local run now prints guest DNS, host authority DNS, and host runner TCP stream numbers from the same public seam operators can invoke directly with `cargo run -p mvm-net --bin mvm-net-bench --features bench -- ...`.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features bench --bin mvm-net-bench -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features bench -- -D warnings`; `cargo run -p mvm-net --bin mvm-net-bench --features bench -- --latency-iterations 100 --authority-iterations 100 --throughput-chunks 64 --payload-bytes 512 --warmup-iterations 16`.

### 2026-07-09 F1 decrypted-stream transform fuzz harness follow-on update

- `mvm-net` now fuzzes the built-in decrypted-stream plugin pipeline as well as the guest packet parser, wire framing, authority core, guest pump, host runner, and host-netd config/stdio seams. `crates/mvm-net/fuzz/fuzz_targets/fuzz_stream_transform_state_machine.rs` drives arbitrary built-in plugin chains, optional secret-binding config, optional response-leak secret values, target hosts, and bounded chunk/finalization cadence through `StreamTransformChain`.
- The harness stays single-crate and runtime-light: it uses only the existing built-in plugin ids plus fixed valid `SecretBinding` inputs, so it can exercise `process()`, `finish_direction()`, `finish()`, validation, and repeated chain reconstruction without live sockets, a TLS handshake, or another crate. Unknown plugin ids and invalid config still fail closed as normal `Err` paths rather than escaping the fuzz boundary.
- The repo’s real fuzz lane now carries the broader `mvm-net` runtime surface too. `.github/workflows/security.yml` now time-boxes every current `mvm-net` fuzz target: guest packet translation, wire framing, host authority, guest pump, host runner, host-netd config/stdio, and the new decrypted-stream transform state machine.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo check --manifest-path crates/mvm-net/fuzz/Cargo.toml`; `cargo clippy --manifest-path crates/mvm-net/fuzz/Cargo.toml --all-targets -- -D warnings`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `rg -n "fuzz_wire_json_authority|fuzz_host_authority_state_machine|fuzz_guest_pump_state_machine|fuzz_host_runner_state_machine|fuzz_host_netd_state_machine|fuzz_stream_transform_state_machine" .github/workflows/security.yml`.

### 2026-07-09 F1 connector-level plugin denial coverage update

- `mvm-net::host_tls` now proves that plugin panic/timeout denials survive the real transformed-TLS seam rather than only the unit layer inside `stream_transform`. Focused connector tests now show a panic during guest plaintext transform and a timeout during flow finalization both surface as guest-visible `TransformError` detail through the TLS connector's live error/close plumbing.
- This slice stays narrow and compile-light: no new runtime policy is introduced, but the existing test-only plugin IDs are now exercised through a real TLS flow so the crash/timeout isolation guarantees are proven at the connector seam where guest-visible failure detail matters.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_surfaces_plugin_panic_denial_detail_during_plaintext_transform --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_surfaces_plugin_timeout_denial_detail_during_flow_finalization --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 F1 per-VM guest request rate-limit slice update

- `mvm-net::host` now enforces a bounded per-VM guest-request rate in the authority core instead of leaving guest-side DNS/TCP-open/UDP/ICMP request volume unbounded within a single VM authority session. `HostAuthorityConfig` now carries `max_guest_requests_per_window` plus `guest_request_rate_window`, defaults both to bounded non-zero values, and rejects zero values at config-build time.
- The limiter is intentionally dependency-light: a fixed-window counter in the authority denies over-budget guest requests with `DenialReason::RateLimited` plus safe detail before policy evaluation or upstream socket/probe work. That keeps the protection inside the single-crate authority core rather than pushing it into a backend-specific runner throttle.
- `mvm-host-netd` now exposes the same knobs in its config surface as `max_guest_requests_per_window` plus `guest_request_rate_window_millis`, rejects invalid zero values during config load, and threads the admitted values into the authority config built for the selected TCP/TLS connector.
- Focused coverage proves the shape across seams: DNS, TCP-open, UDP, and ICMP guest requests all fail closed once the per-VM request budget is exhausted, and a short-window regression test proves the limiter resets after the configured window expires.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd rate_limit --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 F1 built-in plugin buffered-byte enforcement update

- `mvm-net::stream_transform` no longer leaves built-in plugin `max_buffered_bytes` as manifest-only metadata. The plugin wrapper now inspects each built-in plugin's live buffered-byte state after `process()` and `finish()` and denies the flow fail-closed when a plugin buffers more plaintext than its configured budget allows.
- The first enforcement targets the existing single-crate plugin surface instead of adding a new subsystem: `secret-replacement` and `metadata-endpoint-deny` now expose their real buffered-byte state to the wrapper, while non-buffering plugins continue to report zero. This turns the already-documented buffering budgets into actual memory protection.
- Focused coverage uses test-only over-buffer plugins to prove both seams: one plugin exceeds its budget during plaintext transform and one exceeds it during flow finalization, and both now return `PluginDenied` with safe phase detail while the broader stream-transform suite remains green.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd over_buffer --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 F1 host TLS pending-byte backlog cap update

- `mvm-net::host_tls` no longer leaves the guest-facing TLS backlog unbounded within one transformed flow. `HostTlsConnectorConfig` now carries `max_pending_guest_tls_bytes`, defaults it to a bounded non-zero value, rejects zero at config-build time, and each TLS flow now fails closed if the pending guest-facing TLS bytes exceed that configured budget.
- The cap lands on the actual per-flow queue that can grow when the host TLS terminator has produced guest-facing TLS records faster than the caller drains host-to-guest events. Instead of silently retaining those bytes in `pending_guest_bytes`, the connector now records safe detail and returns `TransportError::TransformError` once the budget is exceeded.
- `mvm-host-netd` now exposes the same knob in its config surface as `max_pending_guest_tls_bytes`, rejects zero values during config load, and threads the admitted value into the TLS connector it builds when a per-VM guest intermediate is present.
- Focused coverage proves all three seams: TLS connector config rejects zero budget, `mvm-host-netd` builder and JSON config reject zero budget, and a transformed TLS flow with a deliberately tiny budget fails closed on the first guest handshake flight once the terminator's guest-facing backlog would exceed that budget.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd connector_rejects_zero_pending_guest_tls_budget --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd config_builder_rejects_zero_pending_guest_tls_budget --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd config_json_rejects_zero_pending_guest_tls_budget --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_denies_when_guest_tls_backlog_exceeds_budget --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 F1 upstream TLS plaintext backlog cap update

- `mvm-net::host_tls` no longer lets guest plaintext accumulate unbounded while the upstream rustls client handshake is still in flight. `HostTlsConnectorConfig` now carries `max_pending_upstream_tls_plaintext_bytes`, defaults it to a bounded non-zero value, rejects zero at config-build time, and each transformed flow now fails closed once that host-owned queue would exceed the configured budget.
- The cap lands on the real host-owned queue that appears after guest-side TLS is already established but before upstream TLS is ready to accept application plaintext. Instead of writing unbounded plaintext into the upstream rustls connection during handshake, the connector now buffers that plaintext in a bounded queue, flushes it only after the upstream handshake completes, and defers half-close until the queue is drained or the flow is denied.
- `mvm-host-netd` now exposes the same knob in its config surface as `max_pending_upstream_tls_plaintext_bytes`, rejects zero values during config load, and threads the admitted value into the TLS connector it builds when a per-VM guest intermediate is present.
- Focused coverage proves all three seams: TLS connector config rejects zero budget, `mvm-host-netd` builder and JSON config reject zero budget, and a transformed TLS flow with a deliberately tiny upstream-plaintext budget now fails closed once guest plaintext arrives while the upstream handshake is intentionally stalled.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd connector_rejects_zero_pending_upstream_tls_plaintext_budget --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd config_builder_rejects_zero_pending_upstream_tls_plaintext_budget --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd config_json_rejects_zero_pending_upstream_tls_plaintext_budget --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_denies_when_upstream_tls_plaintext_backlog_exceeds_budget --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 F1 transformed-flow detail budget update

- `mvm-net::host_tls` no longer leaves host-owned transformed-flow detail strings as append-only unbounded state. `HostTlsConnectorConfig` now carries `max_tls_flow_detail_bytes`, defaults it to a bounded non-zero value, rejects zero at config-build time, and every transformed flow now enforces that byte budget across its activity and close-detail accumulation paths.
- The cap lands on the live host-owned detail surfaces that grow as transformed plaintext moves through the built-in plugin chain. Guest/upstream activity detail and close summaries now fail closed with `TransportError::TransformError` once a flow would exceed the configured detail budget, while single stored summaries and error strings are clamped to stay within the same bound instead of retaining arbitrarily long host-generated detail.
- `mvm-host-netd` now exposes the same knob in its config surface as `max_tls_flow_detail_bytes`, rejects zero values during config load, and threads the admitted value into the TLS connector it builds when a per-VM guest intermediate is present.
- Focused coverage proves all three seams: TLS connector config rejects zero budget, `mvm-host-netd` builder and JSON config reject zero budget, and a transformed TLS flow with the built-in `audit` plugin now fails closed once its host-owned detail string would exceed the configured byte budget while still surfacing bounded error detail.

### 2026-07-09 F1 transformed-flow plugin-chain cap update

- `mvm-net::stream_transform` no longer leaves transformed TLS plugin chains as unbounded guest-supplied vectors. The shared pipeline now enforces a small fixed maximum chain length before plugin validation or connector construction, so a hostile route cannot spend host memory or CPU on arbitrarily many repeated built-in plugin entries within one transformed flow.
- The cap lands at the shared plugin-pipeline seam instead of only one caller: `StreamTransformChain::validate_plugin_ids_with_config()` and `StreamTransformChain::from_plugin_ids_with_config_for_target()` now both reject an overlong effective chain after any host-owned auto-appended plugins are accounted for, keeping admission and the connector's defensive open path aligned.
- Focused coverage proves the denial across all three seams: the stream-transform unit layer rejects an overlong chain directly, `HostAuthority` denies an overlong transformed route fail-closed with `DenialReason::PluginDenied`, and the live TLS connector still rejects the same bad route defensively with bounded stored error detail if admission is bypassed.

### 2026-07-09 F1 transformed-flow config-vector cap update

- `mvm-net::stream_transform` no longer leaves host-owned transformed TLS config payloads as unbounded vectors. The shared seam now bounds secret-replacement binding count, explicit secret-replacement value bytes, response-leak-guard secret count, and response-leak-guard total secret-value bytes before plugin validation or connector construction, so a hostile or malformed host-netd config cannot spend host memory on arbitrarily large transform metadata inside one VM authority session.
- The cap lands at the shared transform-config seam instead of only one caller: `validated_effective_plugin_chain()` now validates host-owned `StreamTransformConfig` before building the effective chain, `HostTlsConnectorConfig::build()` rejects invalid transform config eagerly, and `mvm-host-netd` rejects the same bad config through both its builder and JSON load path instead of deferring the failure until a live transformed flow opens.

- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::transformed_tls_route_with_overlong_auto_appended_response_leak_guard_config_is_denied_fail_closed --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 boring guest-TLS internal buffer cap update

- The optional `host-tls-boring` compatibility backend no longer leaves its guest-facing TLS I/O shim with uncapped `Vec<u8>` growth. `BufferedTlsIo` now uses the existing `max_pending_guest_tls_bytes` budget for both inbound guest TLS bytes and outbound guest-facing TLS bytes, so the compatibility path shares the same first per-flow guest-facing pending-byte cap the default rustls path already enforced at the connector seam.
- The cap lands without widening the public config surface: `HostTlsConnectorConfig::max_pending_guest_tls_bytes` remains the single knob, `GuestTlsServer::new()` now threads that existing limit into the Boring-backed guest TLS server only when the compatibility feature is enabled, and budget overruns are normalized back to the same bounded safe detail string so the live connector still surfaces a `TransformError` instead of an opaque Boring I/O failure.

### 2026-07-10 F1 retained TLS flow-detail map cap update

- `mvm-net::host_tls` no longer leaves connector-retained activity/error/close detail maps as unbounded host-owned state. `HostTlsConnectorConfig` now carries `max_retained_tls_flow_details`, defaults it to the same `DEFAULT_MAX_OPEN_FLOWS` budget as the authority's per-VM open-flow cap, and rejects zero at config-build time.
- The cap lands as one shared retained-entry budget across `pending_activity_details`, `pending_error_details`, and `pending_close_details` rather than as another per-map knob. New retained details are tracked in insertion order, and once the connector would retain more entries than the configured budget, the oldest retained detail is evicted instead of leaving host memory growth unbounded after a burst of closed or denied transformed flows.
- `mvm-host-netd` does not grow a new external knob for this slice. When it builds the TLS connector, it now threads the already-admitted `max_open_flows` value into `max_retained_tls_flow_details` so the connector's retained terminal-detail budget stays aligned with the authority's per-VM open-flow cap.
- Focused coverage proves the new seams: TLS connector config rejects zero retained-detail budget, the live connector evicts the oldest retained open-error detail once the shared budget is exceeded, and `mvm-host-netd` wiring now shows the TLS connector inherits its retained-detail cap from `max_open_flows`. Broader `host_tls` and `host_netd` suites stay green too.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd connector_rejects_zero_retained_tls_flow_detail_budget --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_evicts_oldest_retained_open_error_detail_when_budget_is_exceeded --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd_threads_configured_tls_flow_detail_budget_into_tls_connector --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1` (rerun outside the sandbox because the suite binds local sockets); `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1` (rerun outside the sandbox because the suite binds a local Unix socket); `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 retained TLS detail-queue compaction follow-on update

- This closes a real connector-local memory-cap gap that remained after the earlier retained-detail map budget slice: repeated activity/error detail updates on one long-lived transformed flow could keep the queue growing even though the retained maps themselves stayed bounded.
- Focused coverage proves both seams: repeated retained activity-detail replacement now keeps only one queue entry live for that `(flow_id, kind)`, and consuming the retained detail clears the corresponding queue entry too. The broader `host_tls` suite and `host-netd` clippy gate stay green.

### 2026-07-10 F1 connector-local open-flow cap update

- The concrete host TCP connectors no longer trust the authority layer alone to bound connector-local flow maps. `StdTcpConnectorConfig` now carries `max_open_flows`, defaults it to the same `DEFAULT_MAX_OPEN_FLOWS` budget as the authority's per-VM cap, and `StdTcpConnector::open()` now defensively refuses new flows once its internal `streams` map reaches that bound.
- `HostTlsConnectorConfig` now carries the same `max_open_flows` budget, rejects zero at config-build time, and `TlsTerminatingTcpConnector::open()` now fail-closes before allocating another transformed flow once its own `flows` map reaches that bound. The transformed path also stores bounded safe error detail for this defensive denial.
- `mvm-host-netd` does not grow a new external knob for this slice either. It now threads the already-admitted `max_open_flows` value into both the plain TCP connector config and the TLS connector config, so the connector-local open-flow cap stays aligned with the authority's per-VM open-flow cap instead of drifting behind a second operator setting.

Broader `host`, `host_tls`, and `host_netd` suites stay green too.

### 2026-07-10 F1 handshake capability-vector cap update

### 2026-07-10 F1 protocol payload byte-cap update

- `StreamChunk.bytes` and `UdpDatagram.bytes` no longer rely only on guest-side packet parsing discipline, host-side packet synthesis, or the outer authority frame cap for boundedness. `mvm-net::proto` now validates both payload vectors against `MAX_STREAM_CHUNK_BYTES` at the protocol layer.

- DNS authority responses no longer leave their answer vector and TXT-record payloads bounded only by guest packet synthesis at the last moment. `mvm-net::proto` now caps `DnsResponse.answers` and validates `DnsRecordData::Txt` against the same 255-byte single-string limit the guest DNS encoder already enforces.

- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net proto::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest guest_pump::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features wire-json wire_json::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features guest,wire-json -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 DNS wire-shape validation follow-on update

- DNS names and answers no longer rely on guest packet synthesis to discover protocol-invalid wire shapes. `DnsName::new()` now enforces the same total-name and per-label limits the guest DNS encoder already assumes, and `DnsAnswer::validate()` now rejects record-type/data mismatches before a guest packet writer tries to encode them.
- The validation stays at the protocol boundary instead of only in the packet builder. `NetMessage::validate()` now catches malformed DNS names and answer-type mismatches, `GuestBridgePump` now rejects those invalid authority DNS responses before translation, and `LengthPrefixedJsonAuthority` now rejects framed DNS responses that deserialize but still violate those protocol invariants.
- Focused coverage proves the follow-on seam end to end: protocol tests reject empty-label, overlong-label, and record-type/data-mismatch DNS answers directly, guest-pump tests prove a mismatched authority DNS answer now fails before guest packet synthesis, and wire-json tests prove the same malformed DNS response is refused as a protocol-validation error at framed read time.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net proto::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest guest_pump::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features wire-json wire_json::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features guest,wire-json -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 wire-json outbound protocol-validation follow-on update

- `LengthPrefixedJsonAuthority::write_message()` no longer trusts internal callers to hand it only already-validated protocol values. The outbound framing path now runs `NetMessage::validate()` before JSON serialization instead of depending on the read side alone to reject malformed shapes after bytes are already emitted.
- This closes a real bounded-control-plane gap in the opt-in wire adapter: a caller could previously mutate public protocol fields into an overlong `TcpData`, `DnsResponse`, or similar invalid message and make the writer serialize that invalid shape before any frame-size or protocol guard fired. The writer now fails closed before writing any bytes for those invalid messages.
- Focused coverage proves both seams stay independent: a new outbound regression shows an oversized `TcpData` frame is rejected with `JsonWireError::Protocol` before any bytes reach the sink, while the existing inbound-invalid tests now build raw framed bytes directly so the read-path protocol validation still proves decoded invalid frames are refused at framed-read time.
- Validation: `cargo test -p mvm-net --features wire-json wire_json::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features wire-json -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 guest synthetic-host cache cap update

- `mvm-net::guest_packet` no longer leaves the guest-side synthetic-host cache as an unbounded `HashMap<Ipv4Addr, DnsName>`. `GuestPacketTranslatorConfig` now carries `max_synthetic_hosts`, defaults it to the same 65,534-entry envelope as the host authority's default synthetic-DNS pool, and rejects zero at config-build time.
- The cap lands as one bounded cache with generation-safe FIFO eviction rather than as a second per-address knob. New or remapped synthetic-host entries are tracked in insertion order, and once the cache would exceed its configured budget the oldest current entry is evicted instead of letting guest-owned hostname state grow without limit. Stale queue entries from address refreshes are skipped safely, so a remapped address is not dropped accidentally by an older insertion record.
- Focused coverage proves all three seams: guest config now rejects zero synthetic-host budget, overflow evicts the oldest mapping back to literal-IP behavior instead of leaving the cache unbounded, and refreshed addresses do not get evicted by stale queue entries while the newest mapping still drives transformed `:443` routing.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest guest_packet::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features guest -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 guest UDP flow reclamation update

- `mvm-net::guest_packet` no longer leaves admitted guest-side UDP flow state as an append-only `udp_flows`/`udp_flow_keys` pair. The translator still reuses stable flow IDs for repeated guest UDP 4-tuples under normal operation, but now keeps those mappings reclaimable under bounded-memory pressure and terminal host delivery outcomes.
- The cap lands in two places. First, when a new UDP tuple would exceed the shared guest flow budget, the translator evicts the oldest tracked UDP flow instead of permanently consuming the flow budget with append-only unique tuples. Second, `GuestBridgePump` no longer drops `UdpDelivery` blindly: terminal `Denied` and `Failed` delivery statuses now reclaim the guest-side UDP tuple mapping immediately. Reclaimed flow IDs are kept in a bounded tombstone set, so late authority UDP replies for an evicted or terminal flow now drop safely instead of hard-failing the guest pump with `UnknownUdpFlow`.
- Focused coverage proves all three seams: a one-entry guest flow budget now evicts the oldest UDP tuple while still allowing the new tuple through, a late reply for the reclaimed flow is dropped without a guest packet instead of becoming a pump-fatal unknown flow, and a terminal `UdpDelivery::Failed` cleans up the guest-side tuple mapping so the same tuple gets a fresh flow ID on the next outbound datagram.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest guest_packet::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest guest_pump::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features guest -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 guest side-queue compaction follow-on update

- `mvm-net::guest_packet` no longer leaves `synthetic_host_order` and `udp_flow_order` as append-only side queues full of stale references after replacement or removal. Replacing a synthetic-host entry now removes older queue refs for that address before pushing the current generation, and removing a UDP flow now removes that flow id from the FIFO eviction queue before recording the closed-flow tombstone.
- This closes a real guest-side memory-cap gap that remained after the earlier synthetic-host-cap and UDP-flow-reclamation slices: one frequently remapped synthetic address or one repeatedly created-and-failed UDP tuple could keep growing queue state even while the live maps themselves stayed bounded.
- Focused coverage proves both seams: repeated synthetic-host replacement now leaves exactly one live queue entry for that address, and repeated UDP flow create/remove cycles now leave the live UDP maps and their FIFO queue empty instead of keeping stale removal history behind. The broader `guest_packet` suite and guest-feature clippy gate stay green.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest synthetic_host_replacement_does_not_leave_stale_queue_entries --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest udp_flow_removal_does_not_leave_stale_queue_entries --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest guest_packet::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features guest -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 guest closed-TCP tombstone FIFO update

- `mvm-net::guest_packet` no longer bounds recently closed TCP retransmit tombstones with a bare `HashMap` that evicts whichever key happens to appear first in hash iteration order. Closed TCP tombstones are now tracked in explicit FIFO order, so the oldest tombstone is evicted first once the bounded budget is exceeded.
- This closes a real determinism gap in the guest-side retransmit-suppression seam: under unrelated TCP close churn, the translator could previously drop a recently closed flow's tombstone while retaining an older one, making late retransmits fail unpredictably with `UnknownOutboundTcpFlow` instead of being suppressed as an intentionally closed flow.
- Focused coverage proves both seams: when the tombstone budget is exceeded, the oldest closed flow is now evicted first while newer closed flows still suppress retransmits, and repeated reopen/close cycles for one 4-tuple no longer leave stale refs behind in the tombstone-order queue. The broader `guest_packet` suite and guest-feature clippy gate stay green.
- Validation: `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest closed_tcp_flow_budget_evicts_oldest_tombstone_first --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest closed_tcp_flow_reopen_does_not_leave_stale_queue_entries --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest guest_packet::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features guest -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 guest pending-query timeout update

- `mvm-net::guest_packet` no longer leaves pending guest DNS and ICMP authority queries as count-bounded but immortal state. `GuestPacketTranslatorConfig` now carries a bounded non-zero `pending_query_timeout`, and the translator prunes expired pending DNS/ICMP entries before capacity checks and before late response synthesis.
- This closes a real guest-side budget starvation seam: a dropped host authority reply could previously leave a pending-query slot occupied forever, so enough lost DNS or ICMP replies would eventually deny fresh guest queries even though the older work was already unrecoverable.
- Focused coverage proves all three seams: config rejects a zero pending-query timeout, stale pending DNS queries are pruned before a new DNS query would otherwise hit the cap and late DNS replies fail closed as unknown, and the same bounded reclamation now holds for stale pending ICMP echo queries too. The broader `guest_packet` suite and guest-feature clippy gate stay green.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest guest_packet::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features guest -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 host synthetic-DNS TTL reclamation update

- `mvm-net::host` no longer leaves the bounded synthetic-DNS pool as immortal authority state for the full life of a VM. `SyntheticDnsMap` now tracks when each synthetic name was last resolved and prunes entries that have aged past the existing guest-visible DNS TTL before allocating a new synthetic slot.
- This closes a real long-lived-VM exhaustion seam: once enough one-off names had been resolved, the authority could previously hit `SyntheticDnsPoolExhausted` forever even if the guest-side cache should already have aged those names out and no admitted flow still needed the older synthetic slots.
- Focused coverage proves both reclamation paths: a one-slot pool now reuses an expired synthetic entry instead of failing closed on the next unrelated name, and a two-slot pool now reuses a non-tail expired hole without colliding with still-live synthetic entries. The broader host authority suite and host-feature clippy gate stay green.
- Validation: `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host host::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 upstream-root PEM bundle cap update

- `mvm-host-netd` and the TLS connector no longer treat operator-supplied upstream root PEM bundles as merely “non-empty” host-owned config. Explicit `HostTlsUpstreamRoots::PemBundle(...)` input is now bounded by both total byte size and certificate count before connector construction.
- This closes a real host-owned config-shape seam: a very large PEM bundle or a PEM bundle with an excessive number of certificates could previously consume unbounded config memory and parse work before the authority ever opened a transformed TLS flow.
- Focused coverage proves both seams: `HostTlsConnectorConfig` now rejects oversized PEM bundles and overlong certificate sets defensively, and `HostNetdConfig` plus the JSON config loader reject the same malformed upstream-root payloads before daemon startup. The host-netd clippy gate stays green and the focused host-netd/TLS config suites pass.
- Validation: `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd connector_rejects_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd config_builder_rejects_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd config_json_rejects_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 guest-intermediate PEM bundle cap update

- `mvm-host-netd` and the TLS connector no longer treat the host-owned guest TLS intermediate as only “present and non-empty” config. `tls_intermediate` input is now bounded by certificate PEM bytes, private-key PEM bytes, and certificate count before connector construction.
- This closes another real host-owned config-shape seam on the transformed-TLS path: an oversized guest intermediate cert chain or private key could previously consume unbounded config memory and parse work before the authority ever opened a transformed TLS flow.
- Focused coverage proves all three seams: `HostTlsConnectorConfig` now rejects oversized guest-intermediate cert PEM, oversized guest-intermediate key PEM, and overlong guest-intermediate chains defensively, and `HostNetdConfig` plus the JSON config loader reject the same malformed `tls_intermediate` payloads before daemon startup. The host-netd clippy gate stays green and the focused host-netd/TLS config suites pass.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd connector_rejects_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd config_builder_rejects_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd config_json_rejects_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 wire-json hard frame-cap update

- The shared length-prefixed JSON wire adapter no longer treats `max_frame_bytes` as only “non-zero” guest/host runtime config. `JsonWireConfig` now enforces an explicit hard maximum equal to the existing 1 MiB default frame budget, so callers may lower that cap but cannot raise the wire buffer budget arbitrarily.
- This closes a real control-plane and host-owned config seam: `LengthPrefixedJsonAuthority::read_message()` allocates the declared frame body buffer before decoding, so an oversized `max_wire_frame_bytes` setting could previously authorize multi-megabyte or even near-`u32` allocations on both the host authority and guest bridge paths.
- Focused coverage proves the shared and surfaced seams: `JsonWireConfig` rejects frame caps above the hard maximum, `HostNetdConfig` plus the JSON config loader reject oversized `max_wire_frame_bytes` payloads before daemon startup, and `mvm-guest-netd` rejects the same over-budget CLI config through its existing wire-config validation path. The combined clippy gate across the host-netd, guest-linux-runner, and wire-json surfaces stays green.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features wire-json wire_json::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests::config_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest-linux-runner invalid_builder_values_surface_as_config_errors --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features guest-linux-runner,host-netd,wire-json -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 loop message-budget hard-cap update

- The guest and host authority loop-budget knobs no longer treat per-tick/per-run message limits as only “non-zero” runtime config. `GuestPumpLoopConfig` now hard-bounds `max_authority_messages_per_tick`, and `HostRunnerConfig` now hard-bounds `max_messages_per_run`, so callers may lower those fairness budgets but cannot raise them arbitrarily above the existing default ceilings.
- This closes another real host- and guest-owned runtime seam: oversized loop budgets could previously let one readiness tick spend unbounded CPU and queue-drain work inside the guest bridge or host authority loop, delaying fairness and backpressure across the shared vsock authority path even though the knobs were nominally “bounded”.
- Focused coverage proves the shared and surfaced seams: `guest_pump` and `host_runner` now reject over-budget loop configs directly, `HostNetdConfig` plus the JSON config loader reject oversized `max_messages_per_run` payloads before daemon startup, and `mvm-guest-netd` rejects the same over-budget `--max-authority-messages-per-tick` CLI config through its existing pump-config validation path. The combined clippy gate across the affected guest and host loop surfaces stays green.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest guest_pump::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-runner host_runner::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests::config_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest-linux-runner invalid_builder_values_surface_as_config_errors --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features guest,guest-linux-runner,host-runner,host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 authority and translator capacity hard-cap update

- The core authority and guest translator capacity knobs no longer treat long-lived state budgets as only “non-zero” config. `HostAuthorityConfig` now hard-bounds `max_synthetic_dns_entries`, `max_open_flows`, and `max_guest_requests_per_window`, while `GuestPacketTranslatorConfig` now hard-bounds `max_pending_queries`, `max_flows`, and `max_synthetic_hosts`, all at the current default ceilings.
- This closes another real host- and guest-owned memory-cap seam: oversized config values could previously authorize arbitrarily large synthetic-DNS maps, flow tables, rate-limit windows, pending-query tables, and guest-side synthetic-host caches even though the runtime logic otherwise used those knobs as “bounded” state.
- Focused coverage proves the shared and surfaced seams: `host` and `guest_packet` now reject over-budget core configs directly, and `mvm-host-netd` rejects oversized `max_synthetic_dns_entries`, `max_open_flows`, and `max_guest_requests_per_window` payloads through both builder and JSON config load paths before daemon startup. The combined clippy gate across the affected guest, host, and host-netd surfaces stays green.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host host::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest guest_packet::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests::config_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features guest,host,host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 direct connector config hard-cap update

- The remaining direct host TCP/TLS connector builders no longer treat host-owned runtime config as bounded only indirectly by the wider authority and daemon surfaces. `StdTcpConnectorConfig` now rejects zero connect/read timeouts, rejects zero `max_open_flows`, and hard-bounds `max_open_flows` at the same ceiling the host authority already uses. `HostTlsConnectorConfig` now rejects oversized `max_open_flows`, `max_pending_guest_tls_bytes`, `max_pending_upstream_tls_plaintext_bytes`, `max_tls_flow_detail_bytes`, and `max_retained_tls_flow_details` instead of only rejecting zero values.
- This closes another real bypass seam inside the single-crate design: a direct caller could previously sidestep the newer `HostAuthorityConfig` / `HostNetdConfig` hard ceilings and still construct plain or transformed host connectors with invalid zero timeouts or arbitrarily raised flow/detail/backlog budgets even though the production path already treated those same knobs as bounded.
- Focused coverage proves both seams: direct `host` tests now reject invalid plain TCP connector configs and still prove the defensive one-flow open cap at runtime; direct `host_tls` tests now reject oversized transformed-flow budgets; and `host_netd` threading tests plus the host-netd clippy gate prove the surfaced daemon path still constructs valid plain and TLS connectors after the shared builder became fallible.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-std host::tests::std_tcp_connector_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-tls connector_rejects_zero_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-tls connector_rejects_oversized_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests::host_netd_threads_configured_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 host-netd TLS budget hard-cap follow-on update

- `HostNetdConfig` no longer accepts oversized transformed-TLS backlog or detail budgets just because those same values would later be rejected by `HostTlsConnectorConfig`. The daemon builder and JSON config loader now hard-bound `max_pending_guest_tls_bytes`, `max_pending_upstream_tls_plaintext_bytes`, and `max_tls_flow_detail_bytes` at the same ceilings the TLS connector already enforces.
- This closes another real surfaced-config seam: the daemon could previously deserialize and retain arbitrarily large TLS byte-budget values in its own config object even though the later connector build treated those knobs as bounded. The follow-on moves that failure back to config admission, which is the right fail-closed boundary for host-owned runtime config.
- Focused coverage proves both surfaced paths: `HostNetdConfig::builder()` now rejects oversized TLS backlog/detail budgets directly, the JSON config loader rejects the same over-budget payloads before daemon startup, and the `host-netd` clippy gate stays green with the tightened config path.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests::config_builder_rejects_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests::config_json_rejects_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 guest runner poll-timeout hard-cap follow-on update

- `LinuxGuestBridgeRunnerConfig` no longer treats its poll timeouts as only “positive” values. The guest-side runner builder now hard-bounds both `idle_poll_timeout_millis` and `write_poll_timeout_millis` at the existing default 1000 ms ceiling.
- This closes another real guest-owned config seam: a direct caller or `mvm-guest-netd` CLI/env config could previously authorize arbitrarily long `poll(2)` idle/write waits, stretching backpressure and fairness on the shared guest-side authority loop even though those values are operational latency budgets rather than unbounded tuning knobs.
- Focused coverage proves both the direct and surfaced paths: `guest_linux` now rejects oversized poll timeouts at the direct runner builder seam, `guest_netd` rejects the same over-budget CLI config through its existing runner-config admission path, and the guest-linux-runner clippy gate stays green.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest-linux-runner guest_linux::tests::runner_config_rejects_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest-linux-runner guest_netd::tests::oversized_poll_timeout_values_fail_at_runner_config_admission --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features guest-linux-runner -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 stream-transform config constructor fail-closed follow-on update

- `StreamTransformConfig` no longer allows oversized built-in transform config vectors to exist until a later explicit validation call. The public `with_secret_replacement_bindings(...)` and `with_response_leak_guard_secret_values(...)` setters now validate aggregate binding-count and total-secret-byte budgets before returning the config.
- This closes another real host-owned config seam in the transformed TLS path: direct callers could previously construct over-budget secret-replacement or response-leak-guard config and only discover the denial later during plugin-chain validation, TLS connector construction, or host-netd startup. Aggregate config-budget failures now stop at the constructor boundary where the bad config first appears.
- The tighter seam stays intentionally scoped. Aggregate count/byte budgets now fail closed during config construction, while plugin-specific per-secret buffering limits still reject later during transform validation where the plugin-specific budget belongs.
- Focused coverage proves both behaviors: `stream_transform` tests now assert constructor-time denial for over-budget aggregate secret-binding and leak-guard vectors, still assert later fail-closed denial for per-secret response-leak-guard buffering overflow, `host_tls` constructor-path tests now reject the same bad aggregate config before connector build, and the full `host-netd` clippy gate stays green with the fallible config setters threaded through the remaining transformed-TLS call sites.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests::connector_rejects_overlong_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_tls::tests::validate_stream_transforms_rejects_overlong_response_leak_guard_secret_value --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests::transformed_tls_route_with_overlong_auto_appended_response_leak_guard_config_is_denied_fail_closed --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 host authority timeout hard-cap follow-on update

- `HostAuthorityConfig` no longer treats several host-owned timeout knobs as only “non-zero” values. The direct host authority builder now hard-bounds `guest_request_rate_window`, `udp_read_timeout`, `tcp_idle_timeout`, and `udp_idle_timeout` at their existing default ceilings.
- This closes another real host-owned config seam: a direct caller or `mvm-host-netd` config could previously authorize arbitrarily long host-side rate-limit windows or idle/read waits even though those values are fairness and lifecycle budgets on the shared per-VM authority path rather than unbounded tuning knobs.
- The cap now lands at both the shared and surfaced seams. `HostAuthorityConfig::build()` rejects over-budget duration values directly, and `HostNetdConfig` plus the JSON config loader reject the same oversized timeout payloads before daemon startup instead of silently retaining them in host-owned runtime config.
- Focused coverage proves both the direct and surfaced paths: `host` tests now reject oversized guest-request, UDP-read, TCP-idle, and UDP-idle timeout values at the direct authority builder seam; `host_netd` builder and JSON tests reject the same over-budget durations through the daemon config surface; and the `host-netd` clippy gate stays green with the tightened config path.

### 2026-07-11 F1 host DNS TTL hard-cap follow-on update

- `HostAuthorityConfig` no longer treats synthetic-DNS TTL as only a non-zero value. The direct host authority builder now hard-bounds `dns_ttl_seconds` at the existing 30-second default ceiling instead of allowing arbitrarily long synthetic-name retention windows.
- This closes another real host-owned lifecycle seam: a direct caller or `mvm-host-netd` config could previously authorize a much longer synthetic-DNS retention window than the intended per-VM default, stretching both guest-visible cache lifetime and host-side synthetic-name reuse delay even though TTL is a lifecycle budget rather than an open-ended tuning knob.
- The cap now lands at both the shared and surfaced seams. `HostAuthorityConfig::build()` rejects over-budget TTL values directly, and `HostNetdConfig` plus the JSON config loader reject the same oversized `dns_ttl_seconds` payloads before daemon startup instead of silently retaining them in host-owned runtime config.
- Focused coverage proves both the direct and surfaced paths: `host` tests now reject zero and oversized DNS TTL values at the authority builder seam, `host_netd` builder and JSON tests reject the same over-budget payloads through the daemon config surface, and the `host-netd` clippy gate stays green with the tightened synthetic-DNS config path.

### 2026-07-11 F1 guest pending-query timeout hard-cap follow-on update

- `GuestPacketTranslatorConfig` no longer treats pending DNS/ICMP query lifetime as only a non-zero value. The direct guest translator builder now hard-bounds `pending_query_timeout` at the existing 30-second default ceiling instead of allowing arbitrarily long guest-side retention of pending authority query state.
- This closes another real guest-owned lifecycle seam: a direct caller could previously authorize a much longer pending-query timeout than the intended per-VM default, stretching how long expired guest DNS/ICMP slots remained live and delaying bounded state reuse even though that timeout is a lifecycle budget rather than an open-ended tuning knob.
- The cap currently lands at the shared guest translator seam itself. `GuestPacketTranslatorConfig::build()` now rejects over-budget pending-query timeout values directly, so invalid guest-side lifecycle config is denied at construction time instead of surviving until later translator use.
- Focused coverage proves the tightened seam: `guest_packet` config tests now reject oversized pending-query timeout values alongside the existing zero-value and count-limit rejection cases, and the `guest` clippy gate stays green with the new bounded lifecycle config path.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest guest_packet::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features guest -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-11 F1 protocol DNS TTL hard-cap follow-on update

- `mvm-net::proto` no longer treats authority-provided DNS answer TTL as an open-ended guest-visible field. `DnsAnswer::validate()` now hard-bounds `ttl_seconds` at the same 30-second ceiling the host authority already uses for synthetic-DNS answers.
- This closes another real protocol-shape seam in the single-crate design: a malformed or hostile authority response could previously survive the shared protocol layer with an arbitrarily large DNS TTL even though every production host authority answer already intends a short bounded cache lifetime.
- The cap now lands at the shared protocol seam itself. Oversized DNS answer TTL values are rejected during `NetMessage` validation before the guest pump attempts DNS packet synthesis, and the guest-path regression coverage now proves that a decoded authority response with an oversized TTL fails closed before any guest packet bytes are written.
- Focused coverage proves both seams: `proto` tests now reject oversized DNS answer TTL values directly and keep the serde round-trip fixture aligned to the shared 30-second ceiling, `guest_pump` tests now reject oversized authority DNS answer TTL before translation, and the `guest` clippy gate stays green with the tightened protocol-bound validation path.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net proto::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features guest guest_pump::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features guest -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-11 F1 direct TCP timeout hard-cap follow-on update

- `StdTcpConnectorConfig` no longer treats direct TCP connect/read timeouts as only “non-zero” values. The direct connector builder now hard-bounds both `connect_timeout` and `read_timeout` at the existing `tcp_idle_timeout` ceiling instead of allowing arbitrarily large per-flow waits.
- This closes another real direct-caller seam in the single-crate design: a caller that bypassed the higher-level host authority config could still construct a plain TCP connector with effectively unbounded dial/read latency even though those values are lifecycle budgets on the same shared per-VM flow path rather than open-ended tuning knobs.
- The cap now lands at both the shared and surfaced seams. `StdTcpConnectorConfig::build()` rejects over-budget direct TCP timeout values directly, and `HostNetdConfig` plus the JSON config loader reject the same oversized `tcp_connect_timeout` and `tcp_read_timeout` payloads before daemon startup instead of silently retaining them in host-owned runtime config.
- Focused coverage proves both the direct and surfaced paths: `host` tests now reject oversized direct TCP connect/read timeout values at the connector builder seam, `host_netd` builder and JSON tests reject the same over-budget payloads through the daemon config surface, and the `host-netd` clippy gate stays green with the tightened plain-TCP config path.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-std host::tests::std_tcp_connector_config_rejects_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests::config_builder_rejects_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests::config_json_rejects_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-11 F1 benchmark argument hard-cap follow-on update

- The feature-gated `mvm-net-bench` binary no longer treats its public workload knobs as only “non-zero” values. The benchmark parser now hard-bounds latency iterations, authority iterations, throughput chunks, and warmup iterations at their existing default ceilings, and hard-bounds `payload_bytes` at `mvm-net::proto::MAX_STREAM_CHUNK_BYTES`.
- This closes another real tool-surface seam inside the single-crate design: a direct bench invocation could previously authorize arbitrarily large `Vec<u8>` payload allocation and effectively unbounded benchmark work even though the surface is meant to provide compile-light, repeatable measurement of the current public networking seams rather than a general stress harness.
- The cap lands at the public bench CLI seam itself. Oversized benchmark flags are now rejected during argument parsing before any guest-packet, authority, or runner benchmark path allocates the requested payload or begins the requested loop count.
- Focused coverage proves the tightened seam: the bench parser tests now reject oversized `payload_bytes` and iteration counts alongside the existing zero-value denial case, accept explicit in-range overrides, and the `bench` clippy gate stays green with the bounded CLI surface.

### 2026-07-11 F1 audit-chain naming validation follow-on update

- `HostNetdAuditChainConfig` no longer treats signed transparent-network audit metadata as merely present-and-non-empty strings. Audit-chain `tenant` now has to satisfy the repo's existing lowercase tenant-id rules, and `vm_name` now has to satisfy the existing VM-name validator before `mvm-host-netd` accepts the config.
- This closes another real host-owned config-shape seam on the attestable audit path: a caller could previously feed arbitrary tenant/vm label text into the signed transparent-network chain file name and signed event labels even though the rest of the repo already treats those identifiers as bounded safe naming domains.
- The cap lands at the surfaced host-netd audit-config seam itself. Invalid tenant or VM-name shapes are now rejected while decoding the JSON config before the daemon opens the signed audit file or emits any chain-signed event.
- Focused coverage proves the tightened seam: `host_netd` JSON-config tests now reject empty tenant values, invalid tenant-id shapes, and invalid VM-name shapes through the public config loader, and the `host-netd` clippy gate stays green with the tightened audit-config validation path.

### 2026-07-11 F1 stream-transform per-binding/per-secret config-budget follow-on update

- `StreamTransformConfig` no longer treats host-owned secret-replacement binding names and individual response-leak-guard secret values as only aggregate-budget concerns. The shared config constructor now rejects empty secret-replacement binding names, rejects overlong managed placeholders before they can inflate the plugin's live buffering budget arbitrarily, and rejects individual response-leak-guard secret values that would exceed the plugin's buffering ceiling.
- This closes another real transformed-TLS config seam inside the single-crate design: a host-owned JSON/config payload could previously survive construction with a placeholder large enough to authorize much larger `secret-replacement` buffering than intended, or with an individual leak-guard secret that would only be refused later when a transformed flow actually opened.
- The cap now lands at both the shared and surfaced seams. `StreamTransformConfig` fails closed during construction, and `mvm-host-netd` rejects the same empty-name, overlong-placeholder, and overlong-secret-value payloads during JSON config load before daemon startup instead of retaining them in host-owned runtime config.
- Focused coverage proves both seams: `stream_transform` tests now reject empty binding names, overlong managed placeholders, and overlong individual leak-guard secrets directly at config construction time, `host_netd` JSON-config tests reject the same host-owned payloads through the public config loader, and the `host-netd` clippy gate stays green with the tightened transformed-flow config path.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd config_json_rejects_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-11 F1 secret-replacement bound-host pattern validation follow-on update

- `StreamTransformConfig` no longer treats host-owned secret-replacement `target_host` values as opaque strings that only matter later when destination matching runs. The shared config constructor now rejects empty bound hosts, rejects misplaced wildcard syntax, rejects trailing-dot and empty-label shapes, and hard-bounds the underlying host pattern at the same 255-byte hostname ceiling the rest of `mvm-net` already uses.
- This closes another real transformed-TLS config seam inside the single-crate design: a host-owned JSON/config payload could previously survive construction with a malformed or oversized bound-host pattern and only show up later as confusing "no binding matched this destination" behavior when a transformed flow opened.
- The cap now lands at both the shared and surfaced seams. `StreamTransformConfig` fails closed during construction, and `mvm-host-netd` rejects the same empty, malformed-wildcard, and oversized `target_host` payloads during JSON config load before daemon startup instead of retaining them in host-owned runtime config.
- Focused coverage proves both seams: `stream_transform` tests now reject empty bound hosts, malformed wildcard placement, and oversized host patterns directly at config construction time, `host_netd` JSON-config tests reject the same host-owned payloads through the public config loader, and the `host-netd` clippy gate stays green with the tightened bound-host config path.
