### 2026-07-11 F1 secret-replacement empty explicit-value validation follow-on update

- `StreamTransformConfig` no longer leaves explicit empty `secret-replacement` values to be discovered only when a transformed flow opens and the plugin resolves the selected binding. The shared config constructor now rejects any host-owned binding whose inline value is explicitly present but empty.
- This closes another real transformed-TLS config seam inside the single-crate design: a host-owned JSON/config payload could previously survive construction with `value: ""` and only surface later as a live-flow denial, even though the daemon already had enough information to fail that payload closed at config admission time.
- The cap now lands at both the shared and surfaced seams. `StreamTransformConfig` fails closed during construction, and `mvm-host-netd` rejects the same empty explicit-value payloads during JSON config load before daemon startup instead of retaining them in host-owned runtime config.
- Focused coverage proves both seams: `stream_transform` tests now reject empty explicit inline values directly at config construction time, `host_netd` JSON-config tests reject the same host-owned payload through the public config loader, and the `host-netd` clippy gate stays green with the tightened explicit-value config path.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd config_json_rejects_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-11 F1 secret-replacement bound-host whitespace fail-closed correction

- The previous bound-host validation slice had one normalization bug: it pre-trimmed `target_host` before validation, so a host-owned value such as `" api.example.com "` was accepted instead of being rejected as an invalid bound-host pattern.
- `StreamTransformConfig` now validates the original `target_host` string and fails closed on surrounding whitespace rather than normalizing it into a different admission-time host value. This keeps the transformed-TLS binding seam aligned with the rest of the config-hardening posture: host-owned policy strings must already be valid, not "almost valid after trimming."
- The correction lands at both the shared and surfaced seams. `stream_transform` now rejects surrounding-whitespace `target_host` values directly during config construction, and `mvm-host-netd` rejects the same payload during JSON config load before daemon startup instead of silently canonicalizing the host string.
- Focused coverage proves both seams: `stream_transform` tests now reject whitespace-padded bound-host patterns directly at config construction time, `host_netd` JSON-config tests reject the same host-owned payload through the public config loader, and the `host-netd` clippy gate stays green with the corrected fail-closed path.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd config_json_rejects_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-11 F1 response-leak-guard exact-byte preservation follow-on update

- `StreamTransformConfig` no longer trims host-owned `response-leak-guard` secret values during config construction. Those values are treated as opaque bytes for matching/redaction, so leading or trailing whitespace must be preserved exactly instead of being silently rewritten.
- This closes another real transformed-TLS config correctness seam inside the single-crate design: a host-owned JSON/config payload such as `"  sk-live-12345  "` previously became `"sk-live-12345"` during config load, which could make the leak guard redact the wrong bytes and hide a misconfigured secret value.
- The corrected behavior now lands at both the shared and surfaced seams. Exact configured secret bytes are preserved, explicit empty secret values are rejected fail closed during shared config construction, and `mvm-host-netd` rejects the same empty payload during JSON config load before daemon startup.
- Focused coverage proves both seams: `stream_transform` tests now prove whitespace-padded secret values are preserved and matched exactly while empty explicit values are rejected, `host_netd` JSON-config tests reject the same empty host-owned payload through the public config loader, and the `host-netd` clippy gate stays green with the corrected exact-byte path.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd config_json_rejects_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-11 F1 secret-replacement binding-name whitespace fail-closed follow-on update

- `StreamTransformConfig` no longer treats host-owned `secret-replacement` binding names as valid after an implicit trim. The shared config constructor now rejects binding names with surrounding whitespace instead of only trimming them for the emptiness check and then building the real placeholder from the original string.
- This closes another real transformed-TLS config correctness seam inside the single-crate design: a host-owned JSON/config payload such as `" OPENAI_API_KEY "` previously passed validation but produced the placeholder `mvm-managed: OPENAI_API_KEY `, which could silently diverge from the operator-visible binding name they thought they had configured.
- The corrected behavior now lands at both the shared and surfaced seams. `stream_transform` rejects whitespace-padded binding names directly during config construction, and `mvm-host-netd` rejects the same payload during JSON config load before daemon startup instead of storing a silently divergent placeholder.
- Focused coverage proves both seams: `stream_transform` tests now reject surrounding-whitespace binding names directly at config construction time, `host_netd` JSON-config tests reject the same host-owned payload through the public config loader, and the `host-netd` clippy gate stays green with the corrected placeholder-construction path.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd config_json_rejects_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-11 F1 secret-replacement bound-host character validation follow-on update

- `StreamTransformConfig` no longer treats host-owned `secret-replacement` bound-host labels as acceptable just because their wildcard placement, dot layout, and byte lengths are valid. The shared config constructor now also rejects labels containing characters outside ASCII letters, digits, and `-`, and rejects labels that start or end with `-`.
- This closes another real transformed-TLS config correctness seam inside the single-crate design: malformed host text such as `api_exam!ple.com` or `-api.example.com` previously survived config load and only degraded into an inert "this binding never matches any destination" shape later at transformed-flow open time.
- The corrected behavior now lands at both the shared and surfaced seams. `stream_transform` rejects malformed label characters and edge-hyphen labels during config construction, and `mvm-host-netd` rejects the same payloads during JSON config load before daemon startup instead of preserving never-match bindings.
- Focused coverage proves both seams: `stream_transform` tests now reject invalid label characters and edge-hyphen labels directly at config construction time, `host_netd` JSON-config tests reject the same host-owned payloads through the public config loader, and the `host-netd` clippy gate stays green with the tighter hostname-only validation path.

### 2026-07-11 F1 shared secret bound-host validation alignment follow-on update

- The hostname-only bound-host rule is no longer a transformed-TLS-only invariant. `mvm-core::policy::secret_binding` now owns the shared validator for secret destination host patterns, and the older `SecretBinding` parsing path plus the SDK `SecretRef.allowed_hosts` validator both use it.
- This closes a real cross-path drift seam: malformed host text could previously be rejected on the new transparent-TLS `mvm-net` path while still surviving through the legacy secret-binding CLI path or through workload `allowed_hosts` IR validation, leaving different secret-delivery surfaces with different acceptance rules for the same hostname pattern.
- The alignment now lands at all three public seams. CLI-style `SecretBinding` parsing rejects malformed target hosts directly in `mvm-core`, SDK workload validation now emits an explicit `E_SECRET_INVALID_ALLOWED_HOST` error for malformed `allowed_hosts` entries, and `mvm-net` reuses the same shared validator so the transformed-TLS path no longer carries its own forked hostname rule set.
- Focused coverage proves the shared rule across crates: `mvm-core` tests reject malformed target hosts during secret-binding parsing, `mvm-sdk` validation tests and the embedded error-code registry cover invalid `allowed_hosts`, `mvm-sdk` library tests prove the new error code remains explainable, and the `mvm-net` transformed-TLS tests still reject the same malformed host patterns through the host-netd path.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-core secret_binding:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-sdk --test validate -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-sdk --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd stream_transform::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-core --all-targets -- -D warnings`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-sdk --all-targets -- -D warnings`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-11 F1 shared secret bound-host wildcard-match alignment follow-on update

- The shared hostname rule is no longer only about validation. `mvm-core::policy::secret_binding` now also owns the wildcard/exact bound-host match helper, and both the older egress substitution path and the SDK-facing `host_matches` helper delegate to it.
- This closes the adjacent semantic-drift seam: even with shared validation, the legacy substitution path and SDK wildcard helper still had separate implementations of `*.` matching semantics. A future change to one copy could have let validation and runtime matching drift apart again across secret-delivery surfaces.
- The alignment now lands at both runtime and SDK helper seams. `mvm-core::egress_substitution` reuses the shared helper for host-bound substitution decisions, and `mvm-sdk::ir::workload::host_matches` delegates to the same implementation so exact-match, wildcard-subdomain, apex-rejection, and lookalike-guard behavior stay identical.
- Focused coverage proves the shared matcher across crates: `mvm-core` secret-binding tests now cover the helper directly, `mvm-core` egress substitution tests still prove wildcard-bound substitution behavior, `mvm-sdk` workload tests still prove wildcard/lookalike behavior, and the `mvm-core` / `mvm-sdk` clippy gates stay green with the deduplicated helper path.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-core secret_binding:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-core egress_substitution:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-sdk --lib ir::workload::tests::host_matches_handles_wildcards_and_lookalikes -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-core --all-targets -- -D warnings`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-sdk --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-11 F1 host secret-binding metadata allowed-host validation follow-on update

- Host-owned secret-binding metadata no longer leaves `allowed_hosts` validation to the SDK/workload path alone. `mvm-hostd::keyholder::FileBindingStore` now validates every persisted `allowed_hosts` entry with the same shared hostname-only rule used by `SecretBinding`, workload `SecretRef.allowed_hosts`, and the transformed-TLS secret-replacement path.
- This closes the remaining local-metadata drift seam: malformed host text could previously still survive through host-owned binding sidecars or `mvmctl secret set` even after the older secret-binding parser, the SDK validator, and `mvm-net` had aligned on one rule set, leaving the stored host metadata behind the host secret store less strict than the paths that later consumed it.
- The fail-closed behavior now lands at both public host-owned seams. `FileBindingStore::put()` rejects malformed `allowed_hosts` before writing sidecars, `FileBindingStore::get()` rejects stale or manually edited malformed JSON sidecars during reload, and `mvmctl secret set` surfaces the same invalid-host failure detail instead of persisting host metadata that later flows would refuse.
- Focused coverage proves the shared rule across both hostd and CLI seams: `mvm-hostd` binding tests reject malformed `allowed_hosts` on write and on reload from disk, the `mvm-cli` secret-set tests reject the same malformed host text through the public command surface, and the `mvm-hostd` / `mvm-cli` clippy gates stay green with the fail-closed metadata path.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-hostd keyholder::binding:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo test -p mvm-cli commands::ops::secret:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-hostd --all-targets -- -D warnings`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo clippy -p mvm-cli --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 host UDP idle-flow reclamation update

- `mvm-net::host` no longer leaves successfully admitted host-side UDP sockets retained forever just because they have not hit an explicit transport error. `HostAuthorityConfig` now carries `udp_idle_timeout`, defaults it to a bounded non-zero value, rejects zero at config-build time, and `mvm-host-netd` now exposes that same timeout through both its builder and JSON config seams.
- The cap stays transparent to the guest contract. Idle host UDP sockets are pruned before TCP and UDP open-flow admission checks, so abandoned successful UDP sockets cannot starve later TCP or UDP flows under the shared `max_open_flows` budget, and `drain_udp_messages()` also drops expired sockets during its normal housekeeping cadence. A later guest UDP datagram for the same flow id just recreates the host-side socket instead of requiring a new guest-visible close protocol.
- Focused coverage proves the shape across both seams: host authority config rejects zero idle timeout, an expired UDP flow is pruned before a new UDP flow or TCP open would otherwise be denied under a one-flow budget, idle flows are dropped during drain housekeeping, host-netd builder and JSON config reject zero idle timeout, and host-netd threads the configured timeout into the real `HostAuthorityConfig`.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-10 F1 host TCP/TLS idle-flow reclamation update

- `mvm-net::host` no longer leaves admitted TCP/TLS flow state retained forever just because neither side has explicitly closed the stream yet. `HostAuthorityConfig` now carries `tcp_idle_timeout`, defaults it to a bounded non-zero value, rejects zero at config-build time, and `mvm-host-netd` now exposes that same timeout through both its builder and JSON config seams.
- The cap lands in the authority core so one source of truth owns flow lifetime across both plain TCP and transformed TLS. `HostAuthority` now tracks last-activity timestamps for admitted TCP flows, silently prunes expired flows before TCP or UDP open-flow admission checks so stale streams cannot starve new work under the shared `max_open_flows` budget, returns `CloseFlow { HostClosed }` when the guest tries to reuse a flow that has already aged out, and emits close messages during normal drain housekeeping so the guest can reclaim stale TCP/TLS state too.
- Focused coverage proves the shape across both seams: host authority config rejects zero TCP idle timeout, an expired TCP flow is pruned before a second TCP open would otherwise be denied under a one-flow budget, a guest send on an expired flow gets a host-close response instead of a protocol error, drain housekeeping emits close messages for idle flows, host-netd builder and JSON config reject zero TCP idle timeout, and host-netd threads the configured timeout into the real `HostAuthorityConfig`.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-09 F1 chain-signed transparent-network audit update

- `mvm-host-netd` no longer emits only plain structured JSON audit lines. Its config surface now accepts an optional `audit_chain` block, and when present the authority writes a per-VM chain-signed transparent-network audit file under the host signer key while still mirroring the raw structured JSON events to stderr for local debugging.
- The signed chain stays dependency-light and reuse-oriented: it uses the existing host signer key material, the same `prev_hash` plus Ed25519 signature envelope shape the existing verifier understands, and unbound sentinel plan/image values with safe flow-oriented labels (`vm_name`, `transport=transparent_net`, flattened event fields) so the networking authority can produce attestable evidence without linking `mvm-hostd`.
- Backend spawn wiring now writes that audit-chain config for every per-VM transparent-network authority using `mvm_core::config::transparent_net_audit_path(tenant, vm_name)` plus the standard `mvm_keys_dir()/host-signer.ed25519` key path, so signed audit emission is part of the real workload path instead of a unit-test-only seam.
- Focused coverage proves the full slice: `mvm-net` verifies a real signed handshake audit chain with `mvm-verify`, `mvm-host-netd` config rejects invalid audit-chain tenant data, and `mvm-backend`'s authority-spawn config tests pin the emitted audit-chain path/key wiring.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd host_netd::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-backend mvm_net_spawn::tests:: --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-backend --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-14 C3/F1 signed run receipt transparent-network identity widening update

- Signed `mvmctl run --receipt` payloads and the matching JSON summary no longer stop at the CLI-shaped network posture labels. The receipt/schema now carries an explicit `transparent_network_identity` block with the `mvm-net` protocol version, the resolved `mvm-host-netd` binary digest, the embedded `mvm-guest-netd` binary digest, per-host transformed-TLS route plugin manifest digests, and a signed digest of the effective network policy material.
- The identity block is derived from authoritative runtime inputs instead of free-form user text: the host authority digest comes from the same resolved aux binary path the backend would spawn, the guest bridge digest comes from the embedded guest binary bytes that `mvmctl` injects, transformed-TLS route plugin digests come from the runtime `mvm-net::stream_transform` manifest surface for each admitted hostname-bound `:443` route, and the network-policy digest prefers the resolved admitted bundle when one exists.
- This closes a concrete transient-run receipt gap without widening the single-crate networking design: receipts can now identify the transparent-network protocol/binary/plugin/policy surfaces that actually shaped the run, while broader guest-bridge build-provenance threading remains follow-on work.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo test -p mvm-cli configured_transform_route_identities_follow_transparent_tls_chain --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo test -p mvm-cli verify_run_receipt_accepts_valid_signature --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo test -p mvm-cli run_json_summary_omits_raw_output --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo clippy -p mvm-cli --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-14 C3/F1 transient admission build-provenance threading follow-on update

- Transient signed-plan synthesis no longer hardcodes `build_provenance = None` on every `SynthesisInput`. `mvm-core::plan::SynthesisInput` now carries optional `BuildProvenance`, and `synthesize_plan(...)` threads it directly into the signed `ExecutionPlan` so transient admission can bind authoritative artifact identity into the same admitted envelope the receipt code later reads.
- The transient `mvmctl run` / `exec` path now records host-known rootfs and guest-bridge provenance instead of forcing receipt identity to fall back to the local embed table. The admit closure hashes the resolved rootfs once, reuses that digest as both `precomputed_image_sha256` and `build_provenance.artifacts.rootfs`, and still records the embedded `mvm-guest-netd` digest in `artifacts.mvm_netd`. The helper also preserves a more faithful transient source shape: default-image boots stay `NixTemplate`, `--manifest` boots are `LocalProject`, local `rootfs-dir:` boots stay `LocalProject`, and `--image` boots stay `Oci`.
- The OCI-only pre-admission path now also preserves the OCI input pin inside admitted provenance: `input_ref` is the supplied image reference and `lock_digest` is the resolved manifest digest, while receipt generation now prefers `admitted.plan.build_provenance.artifacts.mvm_netd` before any local fallback.
- This narrows the remaining gap from the prior receipt widening slice: the guest-bridge identity recorded in signed transient receipts now comes from the admitted plan itself, and the admitted transient plan now also identifies the exact booted rootfs bytes for the run, while broader full-build provenance population for every transient source remains follow-on work.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo check -p mvm-cli`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo test -p mvm-core carries_build_provenance_into_synthesized_plan --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo test -p mvm-cli guest_bridge_binary_sha256_for_receipt_prefers_admitted_build_provenance --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo test -p mvm-cli transient_build_provenance_carries_rootfs_digest --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo test -p mvm-cli transient_exec_build_provenance_classifies_local_rootfs_dir_source --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo clippy -p mvm-cli --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-14 C3/F1 effective transform-chain capacity planning follow-on update

- `mvm-net::stream_transform::StreamTransformConfig::effective_plugin_chain()` no longer starts from `plugin_ids.to_vec()` and then grows again when host-owned built-ins are auto-appended onto transformed `:443` routes. The effective-chain builder now preallocates from the caller-provided chain length plus the bounded count of auto-appended built-ins, then extends once before the existing ordering adjustments run.
- The same pass also closes a real baseline transformed-TLS regression that the broader host-netd suite exposed while validating the capacity change: `metadata-endpoint-deny` no longer treats any bare short ASCII token such as `ping` as a possible incomplete HTTP method and buffer it indefinitely. The bounded request-head classifier now keeps only real partial HTTP method prefixes sticky, so ordinary non-HTTP plaintext continues upstream immediately even when the baseline chain auto-includes metadata inspection.
- Focused coverage now pins all three semantics that matter on that path: the effective chain still reorders to `secret-replacement -> metadata-endpoint-deny -> response-leak-guard` for a host-owned transformed route, the retained vector starts with the full bounded capacity it needs for those auto-appended built-ins, and the baseline transformed-TLS connector again carries short non-HTTP guest plaintext through the metadata guard to the upstream TLS peer.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd effective_plugin_chain_ --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_passthroughs_short_non_http_ascii_plaintext --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd tls_connector_auto_appends_response_leak_guard_to_baseline_chain --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

### 2026-07-14 C3/F1 split extension-method request-head buffering parity follow-on update

- `mvm-net::stream_transform::metadata-endpoint-deny` now keeps buffering incomplete token-shaped HTTP/1 request-line prefixes once the guest has already sent the method token plus either a request-line separator or a target token, even when the method is an extension verb or a malformed token-shaped verb that must later fail closed.
- This closes the broader transformed-TLS regression the full `host-netd` library sweep exposed after the short-non-HTTP passthrough tightening: split prefixes such as `PROPFIND `, `PROP(FIND `, and `PROP(FIND /computeMetadata/v1/ ` no longer fall out of the bounded candidate path early and leak upstream. They now stay buffered until the next chunk completes enough of the request line to deny as a blocked metadata target or as a malformed request method.
- Focused coverage now pins both shared-seam and connector parity for the three load-bearing cases on that path: valid extension-method prefix plus later metadata host, malformed token-shaped method prefix plus later metadata host, and malformed token-shaped method-plus-target plus later version token, while the positive short non-HTTP plaintext passthrough proof stays green.
- Validation: `cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd split_extension_method_prefix_host_header_metadata_hostname --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd split_invalid_extension_method_prefix --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd split_invalid_extension_method_with_target_only --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test -p mvm-net --features host-netd metadata_endpoint_deny_passthroughs_short_non_http_ascii_plaintext --lib -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo clippy -p mvm-net --all-targets --features host-netd -- -D warnings`.

## Acceptance

- A guest with no network grant cannot resolve names, open TCP, send UDP, or obtain synthetic ICMP echo responses.
- A guest with `--allow-host host:port` can reach only admitted destinations and only through the host authority.
- Secret-bearing HTTPS flows terminate at the transform authority; the guest never receives the real secret value through env, disk, logs, or transformed response bytes.
- Audit records identify DNS decisions, flow opens, flow closes, transform actions, denials, byte counts, and the exact policy digest.
- Receipts identify the `mvm-net` protocol version, host authority binary digest, guest bridge binary digest, transform plugin digests, and effective network policy.
